Cancellation theorems for reciprocity sheaves Thanks: The first author is supported by the Swiss National Science Foundation (SNF), project 200020_178729 Thanks: The second author is supported by JSPS KAKENHI Grant (15H03606).
Alberto Merici, Shuji Saito

Abstract
. We prove cancellation theorems for reciprocity sheaves and cube-invariant modulus sheaves with transfers of Kahn–Miyazaki–Saito–Yamazaki. It generalizes a cancellation theorem for 𝐀 1 \mathbf{A}^{1} -invariant sheaves with transfers, which was proved by Voevodsky. As an application, we get some new formulas for internal hom’s of the sheaves Ω i \Omega^{i} of absolute Kähler differentials.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
19E15 (14F42, 19D45, 19F15)

0.
Introduction

We fix once and for all a perfect field
k
.
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
be the category of separated smooth schemes of finite type over
k
.
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
be the category of finite correspondences:

\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
has the same objects as
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and morphisms in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
are finite correspondences.
Let
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
be the category of additive presheaves of abelian groups on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
, called presheaves with transfers.
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}\subset{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
be the full subcategory of Nisnevich sheaves, i.e.
those objects
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
whose restrictions
F_{X}
to the small étale site
X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
over
X
are Nisnevich sheaves for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.
By a fundamental result of Voevodsky, the inclusion
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}\to{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
has an exact left adjoint
a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
such that for any
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
and
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
,

(a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}F)_{X}
is the Nisnevich sheafication of
F_{X}
as a presheaf on
X_{\operatorname{Nis}}
.
In Voevodsky’s theory of motives, a fundamental role is played by
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariant objects
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
, namely such
F
that
F(X)\to F(X\times\mathbf{A}^{1})
induced by the projection
X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\to X
are isomorphisms for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.
The
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariant objects form a full abelian subcategory
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
that carries a symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
such that

F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G=h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1},{\operatorname{Nis}}}a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}(F\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}G)\;\text{ for }F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}},

where
\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}
is the symmetric monoidal structure on
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
induced formally from that on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
and
h_{0}^{\mathbf{A}^{1},{\operatorname{Nis}}}
is a left adjoint to the inclusion functor
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
, which sends an object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
to its maximal
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariant quotient in
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
.
For integers
n>0
, the twists of
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
are then defined as

F(1)=F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\mathbf{G}_{m},\quad F(n):=F(n-1)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\mathbf{G}_{m};

where
\mathbf{G}_{m}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
is given by
X\to\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}^{\times})
for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.

Noting that
-\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\mathbf{G}_{m}
is an endo-functor on
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, we get a natural map:

(0.1)

\iota_{F,G}:\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F(1),G(1))\;\text{ for }F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}.

One key ingredient in Voevodsky’s theory is the Cancellation theorem
[
15
, Cor, 4.10]
, which implies the following theorem:

Theorem 0.1
.

For
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
,
\iota_{F,G}
is an isomorphism.

The purpose of this paper is to generalize the above theorem to reciprocity sheaves.
The category
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
of reciprocity sheaves was introduced in
[
5
]
and
[
6
]
as a full subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
that contains
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
as well as interesting non-
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariant objects such as the additive group scheme
\mathbf{G}_{a}
, the sheaf of absolute Kähler differentials
\Omega^{i}
and the de Rham-Witt sheaves
W_{n}\Omega^{i}
.
In
[
10
]
, a lax monoidal structure
(\_\;,\_)_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}

is defined in such a way that

(F,G)_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}=F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G\;\text{ for }F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}.

It allows us to define the twists for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
recursively as

F\langle 1\rangle:=(F,\mathbf{G}_{m})_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}},\quad F\langle n\rangle:=(F\langle n-1\rangle,\mathbf{G}_{m})_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}.

Some examples of twists were computed in
[
10
]
:
if
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, then
F\langle n\rangle=F(n)
, in particular

\mathbb{Z}\langle n\rangle\cong\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}
(the Milnor
K
-sheaf),
and
\mathbf{G}_{a}\langle n\rangle\cong\Omega^{n}
if
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
.

By the fact that
(-,\mathbf{G}_{m})_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}
is an endo-functor on
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, we get a natural map (cf. (
5.14
)) :

(0.2)

\iota_{F,G}:\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle)\;\text{ for }F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}},

which coincides with (
0.1
) if
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
.
We will also get a natural map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
:

(0.3)

\lambda_{F}:F\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},F\langle n\rangle)\;\text{ for }F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}},

using the functoriality of
(-,\mathbf{G}_{m})_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, where
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}
denotes the internal hom in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
.

The main result of this paper is the following:

Theorem 0.2

(Theorems
5.4
and
5.2
)
.

The maps
\iota_{F,G}
and
\lambda_{F}
are isomorphisms.

As an application of the above theorem, we prove the following.

Corollary 0.3
.

(Theorem
6.2
)
Assume
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
. For integers
m,n\geq 0
, there are natural isomorphisms in
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
:

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})\cong\Omega^{m-n}\oplus\Omega^{m-n-1},

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},\Omega^{m})\cong\Omega^{m-n},

where
\Omega^{i}=0
for
i<0
by convention.

Let
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}
be the category of additive presheaves of abelian groups on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
(without transfers).
Note that
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
is viewed as a subcategory of
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}
.
By a lemma due to Kay Rülling (see Lemma
1.1
), we have a natural isomorphism in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}
:

(0.4)

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(G,\Omega^{m})\cong\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(G,\Omega^{m})\;\text{ for any }G\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}},

where
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}
is the internal hom in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}
.
Thanks to (
0.4
), the isomorphisms of Corollary
0.3
and its explicit descriptions (
6.1
) and (
6.3
) imply

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})=\{\omega_{1}\wedge(-)+\omega_{2}\wedge d(-)\;|\;\omega_{1}\in\Omega^{m-n}_{k},\;\omega_{2}\in\Omega^{m-n-1}_{k}\},

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},\Omega^{m})=\{\omega\wedge{\operatorname{dlog}}(-)\;|\;\omega\in\Omega^{m-n}_{k}\},

where
{\operatorname{dlog}}:\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}\to\Omega^{m}
is the map

\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\}\to{\operatorname{dlog}}x_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge{\operatorname{dlog}}x_{n}
.
It would be an interesting question if there is a direct proof of these formulas which does not use the machinery of modulus sheaves with transfers explained below.

Reciprocity sheaves are closely related to
modulus sheaves with transfers

introduced in
[
3
]
and
[
4
]
: Voevodsky’s category
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
of finite correspondences is enlarged to a new category
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
of
modulus pairs
: Its objects are pairs
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)
where
X
is a separated scheme of finite type over
k
and
D
is an effective Cartier divisor on
X
such that
\mathcal{X}^{\circ}:=X-|D|\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
(
\mathcal{X}^{\circ}
is called the interior of
\mathcal{X}
).
The morphisms are finite correspondences on interiors satisfying some admissibility and properness conditions.
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
be the full subcategory of such objects
(X,D)

that
X
is proper over
k
.
There is a symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
, which also induces that on
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
by restriction (cf. §
1
(
19
)) .

We then define
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
) as the category of additive presheaves of abelian groups on
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
).
We have a functor

\underline{\omega}:\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}\;;\;({\overline{X}},X_{\infty})\to{\overline{X}}-|X_{\infty}|,

and two adjunctions

\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}\begin{smallmatrix}\tau^{*}\\
\longleftarrow\\
\tau_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\end{smallmatrix}\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}},\quad\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}\begin{smallmatrix}\underline{\omega}^{*}\\
\longleftarrow\\
\underline{\omega}_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\end{smallmatrix}{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}},

where
\underline{\omega}^{*}
is induced by
\underline{\omega}
and
\underline{\omega}_{!}
is its left Kan extension, and
\tau^{*}
is induced by the inclusion
\tau:\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
and
\tau_{!}
is its left Kan extension, which turned out to be exact and fully faithful.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
write
F_{\mathcal{X}}
for the presheaf
on the small étale site
X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
over
X
given by
U\to F(\mathcal{X}_{U})
for
U\to X
étale, where
\mathcal{X}_{U}=(U,D\times_{X}U)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
.
We say
F
is a Nisnevich sheaf if so is
F_{\mathcal{X}}
for all
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
.
We write
\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
for the full subcategory of Nisnevich sheaves.

The replacement of the
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariance in this new framework is
the
{\overline{\square}}
-invariance, where
{\overline{\square}}:=(\mathbf{P}^{1},\infty)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
:
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
be the full subcategory of those objects

F
that
F(\mathcal{X})\to F(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}})
induced by the projection

\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}\to\mathcal{X}
are isomorphisms for all

\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
. Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
be the essential image of
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
under
\tau_{!}
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
be the full subcategory of
semipure
objects
F
, namely such objects that the natural map

F(X,D)\to F(X-D,\emptyset)
are injective for all
(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
.
We also define
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}=\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}\cap\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
as a full subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
.
A symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}
(resp.
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}
)
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
(resp. on
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
) can be defined in the same spirit as
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
(see §
3
).

The relationship between reciprocity (pre)sheaves and
{\overline{\square}}
-invariant modulus (pre)sheaves with transfers is encoded in

\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}=\underline{\omega}_{!}(\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp})\quad\text{ and }\quad\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}=\underline{\omega}_{!}(\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}).

There is a pair of adjoint functors

\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}\begin{smallmatrix}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\\
\longleftarrow\\
\underline{\omega}_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\end{smallmatrix}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\begin{smallmatrix}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\\
\longleftarrow\\
\underline{\omega}_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\end{smallmatrix}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}

such that
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F=\underline{\omega}^{*}F
for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}
.
Moreover, the lax monoidal structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
is induced by the symmetric monoidal structure on

\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
via the formula:

(F,G)_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}}:=\underline{\omega}_{!}(\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G)\text{ for }\;\;F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}.

The endo-functor
-\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
induces a natural map for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
:

(0.5)

\iota_{F}:F\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}),

where
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}
denotes the internal hom in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
.
Now Theorem
0.2
will be a consequence of the following result:

Theorem 0.4

(Cor
3.6
)
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
and
\widetilde{F}=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, the map

\iota_{\widetilde{F}}
is an isomorphism.

We give an outline of the content of the paper:

•

In section
1
we first review basic definitions and results of the theory of modulus (pre)sheaves with transfers and reciprocity sheaves from
[
3
]
,
[
4
]
and
[
13
]
.
We also prove some technical lemmas which will be used in
the later sections.

•

In section
2
we define the contraction functors
\gamma
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, which generalize Voevodsky’s contraction functors on
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
(cf.
[
7
, Lecture 23]
) to the setting of modulus (pre)sheaves with transfers. We prove some technical lemmas which will be used in the later sections.

•

In section
3
we define the symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}
(resp.
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}
) on
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
(resp. on
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
) using results from section
1
.
The endo-functor
-\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
induces a natural map for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
:

(0.6)

\iota_{F}:F\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}).

We state the main Theorem
3.4
:
\iota_{F}
is an isomorphism.
Theorem
0.4
is deduced from it by using results from sections
2
.

The last half of the section is devoted to the proof of the split-injectivity of the map

\iota_{F}
(
0.6
). In order to construct a section of
\iota_{F}
, we follow the same strategy as
[
15
]
by generalizing the techniques used in loc. cite.

•

In section
4
we finish the proof of Theorem
3.4
by showing the surjectivity of
\iota_{F}
. We again follow the same strategy as
[
15
]
by generalizing the results of
[
16
, Section 2.7]
: here a technical problem is that for
(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
, the diagonal map
X\to X\times X
does not induce a map
(X,D)\to(X,D)\otimes(X,D)
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
but only induces a map

(X,2D)\to(X,D)\otimes(X,D)
, where
2D\hookrightarrow X
is the thickening of

D\hookrightarrow X
defined by the square of the ideal sheaf.
This is the main reason why we need to work with
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
instead of
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}

employing much more intricate arguments than those in
[
15
]
and

[
16
, Section 2.7]
, for which we need the technical results in §
1
and §
2
.

•

In section
5
we deduce Theorem
0.2
from Theorem
0.4
.

•

In section
6
we deduce Corollary
0.3
from Theorem
0.2
.

Conventions

In the whole paper we fix a perfect base field
k
.
Let
\widetilde{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}}
be the category of
k
-schemes
X
which are essentially smooth over
k
, i.e.
X
is a limit
\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{i\in I}X_{i}
over a filtered set
I
,
where
X_{i}
is smooth over
k
and all transition maps are étale.
Note
\operatorname{Spec}K\in\widetilde{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}}
for a function field
K
over
k
thanks to the assumption that
k
is perfect.
We frequently allow
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
to take values
on objects of
\widetilde{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}}
by

F(X):=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i\in I}F(X_{i})
for
X
as above.

1.
Recollection on modulus sheaves with transfers

In this section we recall the definitions and basic properties of modulus sheaves with transfers from
[
3
]
and
[
13
]
(see also
[
6
]
for a more detailed summary).

(1)

Denote by
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}
the category of separated schemes of finite type over
k
and by
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
the full subcategory of smooth schemes. For
X,Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, an integral closed subscheme of
X\times Y
that is finite and surjective over a connected component of
X
is called a
prime correspondence from
X
to
Y
.
The category
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
of finite correspondences has the same objects as
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, and for
X,Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
,
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X,Y)
is the free abelian group on the set of all prime correspondences from
X
to
Y
(see
[
7
]
). We consider
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
as a subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
by regarding a morphism in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
as its graph in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
.

Let
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}=Fun(\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}},\operatorname{\mathbf{Ab}})
be the category of additive presheaves of abelian groups on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
whose objects are called
presheaves with transfers
.
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}\subseteq{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
be the category of Nisnevich sheaves with transfers and let

(1.1)

a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{V}:{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}

be Voevodsky’s Nisnevich sheafification functor, which is an exact left adjoint to the inclusion
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}\to{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
.
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}\subseteq{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
be the category of
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariant presheaves and put
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}=\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}\cap\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
.
The product
\times
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
yields a symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
,
which induces a symmetric monoidal structure on
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
in the usual way.

(2)

We recall the definition of the category
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
from
[
3
, Definition 1.3.1]
. A pair
\mathcal{X}=(X,D_{X})
of
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}
and an effective Cartier divisor
D
on
X
is called a
modulus pair
if
X-|D_{X}|\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.
Let
\mathcal{X}=(X,D_{X})
,
\mathcal{Y}=(Y,D_{Y})
be modulus pairs and
\Gamma\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X-D_{X},Y-D_{Y})
be a prime correspondence. Let
\overline{\Gamma}\subseteq X\times Y
be the closure of
\Gamma
, and let
\overline{\Gamma}^{N}\to X\times Y
be the normalization. We say
\Gamma
is
admissible
(resp.
left proper
) if
(D_{X})_{\overline{\Gamma}^{N}}\geq(D_{Y})_{\overline{\Gamma}^{N}}

(resp. if
\overline{\Gamma}
is proper over
X
). Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
be the
subgroup of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X-D_{X},Y-D_{Y})
generated by all admissible left proper prime correspondences. The category
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
has modulus pairs as objects and
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
as the group of morphisms from
\mathcal{X}
to
\mathcal{Y}
.

(3)

Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
be the full subcategory of
(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
with
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and
|D|
a simple normal crossing divisor on
X
. As observed in
[
13
, Remark 1.14]
, after assuming resolution of singularities, we can assume
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}\cong\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
, as for every object
(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
there exists a proper birational map
p:X^{\prime}\to X
that is an isomorphism on
X-|D|
and such that
|p^{*}D|
is a simple normal crossing divisor. Hence the modulus correspondence
(X^{\prime},D^{\prime})\to(X,D)
induced by the graph of
p
is invertible in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
.

(4)

There is a canonical pair of adjoint functors
\lambda\dashv\underline{\omega}
:

\lambda:\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}\quad X\mapsto(X,\emptyset),

\underline{\omega}:\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}\quad(X,D)\mapsto X-|D|,

(5)

There is a full subcategory
\mathbf{MCor}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
consisting of

proper modulus pairs
, where
a modulus pair
(X,D)
is
proper
if
X
is proper.
Let
\tau:\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
be the inclusion functor and
\omega=\underline{\omega}\tau
.

(6)

For all
n>0
there is an endofunctor
(\_)^{(n)}
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
preserving
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
,
such that
(X,D)^{(n)}=(X,nD)
where
nD
is the
n
-th thickening of
D
.

(7)

We have two categories of
modulus presheaves with trasnfers
:

\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}=Fun(\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}},\mathbf{Ab})\text{ and }\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}=Fun(\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}},\mathbf{Ab}).

Let
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{X})=\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}(-,\mathcal{X})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
be the representable presheaf for

\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
. In this paper we frequently write
\mathcal{X}
for
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{X})
for simplicity.

(8)

The adjunction
\lambda\dashv\underline{\omega}
induce a string of
4
adjoint functors
(\lambda_{!}=\underline{\omega}^{!},\lambda^{*}=\underline{\omega}_{!},\lambda_{*}=\underline{\omega}^{*},\underline{\omega}_{*})
(cf.
[
3
, Pr. 2.3.1]
):

\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}\begin{smallmatrix}\underline{\omega}^{!}\\
\longleftarrow\\
\underline{\omega}_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\underline{\omega}^{*}\\
\longleftarrow\\
\underline{\omega}_{*}\\
\longrightarrow\end{smallmatrix}{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}

where
\underline{\omega}_{!},\underline{\omega}_{*}
are localisations and
\underline{\omega}^{!}
and
\underline{\omega}^{*}
are fully faithful.

(9)

The functor
\omega
yields a string of
3
adjoint functors

(\omega_{!},\omega^{*},\omega_{*})
(cf.
[
3
, Pr. 2.2.1]
):

\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}\begin{smallmatrix}\omega_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\omega^{*}\\
\longleftarrow\\
\omega_{*}\\
\longrightarrow\end{smallmatrix}{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}

where
\omega_{!},\omega_{*}
are localisations and
\omega^{*}
are fully faithful.

(10)

The functor
\tau
yields a string of
3
adjoint functors
(\tau_{!},\tau^{*},\tau_{*})
:

\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}\begin{smallmatrix}\tau_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\tau^{*}\\
\longleftarrow\\
\tau_{*}\\
\longrightarrow\end{smallmatrix}\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}

where
\tau_{!},\tau_{*}
are fully faithful and
\tau^{*}
is a localisation;
\tau_{!}

has a pro-left adjoint
\tau^{!}
, hence is exact (cf.
[
3
, Pr. 2.4.1]
). We will denote by
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}^{\tau}
the essential image of
\tau_{!}
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
. Moreover, we have (cf.
[
3
, Lem. 2.4.2]
)

(1.2)

\omega_{!}=\underline{\omega}_{!}\tau_{!},\;\omega^{*}=\tau^{*}\underline{\omega}^{*},\;\tau_{!}\omega^{*}=\underline{\omega}^{*}.

(11)

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
and
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
, write
F_{\mathcal{X}}
for the presheaf
on the small étale site
X_{{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
over
X
given by
U\to F(\mathcal{X}_{U})
for
U\to X
étale, where
\mathcal{X}_{U}=(U,D_{|U})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
. We say
F
is a Nisnevich sheaf if so is
F_{\mathcal{X}}
for all
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
(see
[
3
, Section 3]
).
We write
\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
for the full subcategory of Nisnevich sheaves.
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{MNST}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
be the full subcategory of such objects
F
that
\tau_{!}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
.
By
[
3
, Prop. 3.5.3]
and
[
4
, Theorem 2]
, the inclusion functors

{\underline{i}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}:\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}\quad\text{ and }\quad i_{\operatorname{Nis}}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MNST}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}

admit exact left adjoints
\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
respectively and there are natural isomorphisms

(1.3)

\tau_{!}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\simeq\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\tau_{!}\quad\text{ and }\quad a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\tau^{*}\simeq\tau^{*}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}},

and the adjunction from (
10
) induces an adjunction

\operatorname{\mathbf{MNST}}\begin{smallmatrix}\tau_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\tau^{*}\\
\longleftarrow\end{smallmatrix}\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}.

The functor
\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
has the following description: For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
and
\mathcal{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
, let
F_{\mathcal{Y},{\operatorname{Nis}}}
be the usual Nisnevich sheafification of
F_{\mathcal{Y}}
.
Then, for
(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
we have

(1.4)

\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F(X,D)=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{f:Y\to X}F_{(Y,f^{*}D),{\operatorname{Nis}}}(Y),

where the colimit is taken over all proper maps
f:Y\to X
that induce isomorphisms
Y-|f^{*}D|\xrightarrow{\sim}X-|D|
.

(12)

For
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}
, let
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\operatorname{Nis}},\operatorname{\mathbf{Ab}})
be the abelian category of additive sheaves on
X_{\operatorname{Nis}}
.
By definition of
\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
, we have an additive functor for
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
,

\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\operatorname{Nis}},\operatorname{\mathbf{Ab}})\;;\;F\to F_{\mathcal{X}}.

The functor is not exact in general but it is left exact by (
1.4
).

(13)

By
[
4
, Pr. 6.2.1]
, the functors
\underline{\omega}^{*}
and
\underline{\omega}_{!}
respect
\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
, and induce a pair of adjoint functors

\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}\begin{smallmatrix}\\
\underline{\omega}_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\underline{\omega}^{*}\\
\longleftarrow\end{smallmatrix}\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}},

which are both exact. Moreover, we have

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}=a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{V}\underline{\omega}_{!}\quad\text{ and }\quad\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}=\underline{\omega}^{*}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{V}.

(14)

We say that
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
) is
semi-pure
if the unit map

u:F\to\underline{\omega}^{*}\underline{\omega}_{!}F\quad(\text{resp. }u:F\to\omega^{*}\omega_{!}F)

is injective. For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
(resp.
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
), let
F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
(resp.
F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
) be the image of

F\to\underline{\omega}^{*}\underline{\omega}_{!}F
(resp.
F\to\omega^{*}\omega_{!}F
) (called the semi-purification of
F
). One easily sees that the association
F\to F^{sp}
gives a left adjoint to the inclusion of the full subcategories of semipure objects into
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
. For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
we have

(1.5)

\tau_{!}(F^{sp})\simeq(\tau_{!}F)^{sp}.

This follows from the fact that
\tau_{!}
is exact and commutes with

\omega^{*}\omega_{!}
and
\underline{\omega}^{*}\underline{\omega}_{!}

since
\tau_{!}\omega^{*}=\underline{\omega}^{*}
and
\underline{\omega}_{!}\tau_{!}=\tau_{!}
(cf. (
10
)).
In particular
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
is semiupre if and only if so is
\tau_{!}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
.
For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
we have

(1.6)

\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(F^{sp})\simeq(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F)^{sp},

where the
(\_)^{sp}
on the right is defined for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
in the same way as above. This follows from the fact that
\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
is exact and commutes with

\omega^{*}\omega_{!}
and
\underline{\omega}^{*}\underline{\omega}_{!}
(cf. (
13
)).

(15)

Let
{\overline{\square}}:=(\mathbf{P}^{1},\infty)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
.
We say
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
is
{\overline{\square}}
-invariant if
p^{*}:F(\mathcal{X})\to F(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}})
is an isomorphism for any
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
, where
p:\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}\to\mathcal{X}
is the projection.
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
consisting of all
{\overline{\square}}
-invariant objects.

Recall from
[
6
, Theorem 2.1.8]
that
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
is a Serre subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
,
and that the inclusion functor
i^{\overline{\square}}:\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}

has a left adjoint
h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}
and a right adjoint
h^{0}_{\overline{\square}}
given
for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
and
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
by

\displaystyle h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(F)(\mathcal{X})=\operatorname{Coker}(i_{0}^{*}-i_{1}^{*}:F(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}})\to F(\mathcal{X})),

\displaystyle h^{0}_{\overline{\square}}(F)(\mathcal{X})=\operatorname{Hom}(h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathcal{X}),F),

where for
a\in k
the section
i_{a}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}
is induced by the map
k[t]\to k[t]/(t-a)\cong k
.

For
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
, we write
h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathcal{X})=h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{X}))\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
.

(16)

Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}=\tau_{!}\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
be the essential image of
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
under
\tau_{!}
.
In this paper, for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
, we let
F
denote also
\tau_{!}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
by abuse of notation.
Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
) be the full subcategory of semipure objects. By (
1.5
), we have

(1.7)

F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}\text{ for }\;\;F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau},

and
\tau_{!}
and
\tau^{*}
induce an equivalence of categories

(1.8)

\tau_{!}:\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}\simeq\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}:\tau^{*}

with natural isomorphisms
\tau^{*}\tau_{!}\simeq id
and
\tau_{!}\tau^{*}\simeq id
.

We also consider the full subcategories

\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}=\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}\cap\operatorname{\mathbf{MNST}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{MNST}},

\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}=\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}\cap\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}.

\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}=\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}\cap\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}.

By
[
13
, Th. 0.4]
, we have

(1.9)

\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp})\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}.

By
[
4
, Th. 2 (1)]
,
\tau_{!}
and
\tau^{*}
induce an equivalence of categories

(1.10)

\tau_{!}:\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\simeq\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}:\tau^{*}

with natural isomorphisms
\tau^{*}\tau_{!}\simeq id
and
\tau_{!}\tau^{*}\simeq id
.

(17)

We write
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}\subseteq{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
for the essential image of
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
under

\omega_{!}
(which is the same as the essential image of
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
under

\underline{\omega}_{!}
since
\omega_{!}=\underline{\omega}_{!}\tau_{!}
and
\underline{\omega}_{!}F=\underline{\omega}_{!}F^{sp}
).
Put
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}=\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}\cap\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
.
The objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
) are called reciprocity presheaves
(resp. sheaves).
We have
\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}
and it contains also smooth commutative group schemes (which may have non-trivial unipotent part), and the sheaf
\Omega^{i}
of Kähler differentials, and the de Rham-Witt sheaves
W\Omega^{i}
(see
[
5
]
and
[
6
]
).

(18)

By
[
6
, Prop. 2.3.7]
we have a pair of adjoint functors:

(1.11)

\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}\begin{smallmatrix}\omega_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\\
\longleftarrow\end{smallmatrix}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}},

where
\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}=h^{0}_{\overline{\square}}\omega^{*}
and it is fully faithful.
It induces a pair of adjoint functors:

(1.12)

\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}\begin{smallmatrix}\underline{\omega}_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\\
\longleftarrow\end{smallmatrix}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}},

where
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}=\tau_{!}h^{0}_{\overline{\square}}\omega^{*}
and it is fully faithful.
Indeed, let
F=\tau_{!}\hat{F}
for
\hat{F}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
and
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}
.
In view of (
15
) and the exactness and full faithfulness of
\tau_{!}
, we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}}(F,\tau_{!}h^{0}_{\overline{\square}}\omega^{*}G)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}(\hat{F},h^{0}_{\overline{\square}}\omega^{*}G)\simeq\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(\hat{F},\omega^{*}G)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\tau_{!}\hat{F},\underline{\omega}^{*}G)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}}(\underline{\omega}_{!}F,G).

By
[
6
, Theorem 2.4.1(2)]
, (
1.12
) induce a pair of adjoint functors:

(1.13)

\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\begin{smallmatrix}\underline{\omega}_{!}\\
\longrightarrow\\
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\\
\longleftarrow\end{smallmatrix}\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}},

If
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
, the adjunction induces a canonical map

F\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F

which is injective if
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
.

(19)

\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
is equipped with a symmetric monoidal structure given by

(X,D_{X})\otimes(Y,D_{Y}):=(X\times Y,D_{X}\times Y+X\times D_{Y}),

and
\mathbf{MCor}
is clearly a
\otimes
-subcategory. Notice that the product is not a categorical product since the diagonal map is not admissible. It is admissible as a correspondence

(X,D_{X})^{(n)}\to(X,D_{X})\otimes(X,D_{X})\qquad\text{for }n\geq 2

The symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
) induces a symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
) in the usual way, and

\tau_{!}
,
\omega_{!}
and
\underline{\omega}_{!}
from (
10
), (
9
) and (
8
) are all monoidal (see
[
10
, §3]
).

We end this section with some lemmas that will be needed in the rest of the paper.

The proof of the following Lemma is due to Kay Rülling. We thank him for letting us include it in our paper.

Lemma 1.1
.

Let
p
be the exponential characteristic of the base field
k
.
Let
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
such that

(1)

for all dominant étale maps
U\to X
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}

the pullback
F(X)\to F(U)
is injective,

(2)

F
has no
p
-torsion.

Then, for any
G\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
, the natural map

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(G,F)\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(G,F)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(Kay Rülling)
First we prove
\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(G,F)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(G,F)
, i.e. any morphism
\varphi:G\to F
of presheaves on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
is also a morphism in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
.
We have to show
\varphi(f^{*}a)=f^{*}\varphi(a)
in
F(X)
,
for
a\in G(Y)
and
f\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X,Y)
a prime correspondence.
By (1) we can reduce to the case
X=\operatorname{Spec}K
, with
K
a function field over
k
.
In this case we can write
f^{*}=h_{*}g^{*}
, where
h:\operatorname{Spec}L\to\operatorname{Spec}K
is induced by a finite field extension
L/K

and
g:\operatorname{Spec}L\to Y
is a morphism.
Since
\varphi
is a morphism of presheaves on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
,
we are reduced to show

(1.14)

h_{*}\varphi(a)=\varphi(h_{*}a),\quad a\in G(L).

It suffices to consider the following two cases:

1st case: L/K is finite separable.

Let
E/K
be a finite Galois extension containing
L/K
and denote by
j:\operatorname{Spec}E\to\operatorname{Spec}K
the induced morphism and
by
\sigma_{i}:\operatorname{Spec}E\to\operatorname{Spec}L
the morphism induced by all
K
-embeddings of
L
into
E
.
Since
G\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
we obtain in
G(E)

j^{*}h_{*}a=(h^{t}\circ j)^{*}a=\sum_{i}\sigma_{i}^{*}(a).

Thus

j^{*}\varphi(h_{*}a)=\varphi(j^{*}h_{*}a)=\varphi(\sum_{i}\sigma_{i}^{*}(a))=\sum_{i}\sigma_{i}^{*}\varphi(a)=j^{*}h_{*}\varphi(a).

Since
j^{*}:F(L)\to F(E)
is injective by (1) this shows (
1.14
) in this case.

2nd case: L/K is purely inseparable of degree
p
.

In this case we have
h^{*}h_{*}=(h^{t}\circ h):G(L)\to G(L)
is multiplication by
p
as well as

h_{*}h^{*}:G(K)\to G(K)
.
Thus

h^{*}\varphi(h_{*}a)=\varphi(h^{*}h_{*}a)=p\varphi(a)=h^{*}h_{*}\varphi(a);

applying
h_{*}
yields

p\varphi(h_{*}a)=ph_{*}\varphi(a);

thus (
1.14
) follows from (2).

Next we prove the analogous statement for internal hom’s.
Indeed, note that for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
,
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X),F)\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
also satisfies (1) and (2) above and that we have

(1.15)

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(X),F)=F(X\times-)=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(h_{X},F)\quad\text{in }{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}},

where
h_{X}=\mathbb{Z}(\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}}(-,X))
.
Thus for
G\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}

\displaystyle\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(G,F)(X)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(G,F))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(G\otimes^{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),F)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(G,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),F))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(G,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),F)),

by
(
1.14
)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(G,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(h_{X},F)),

by
(
1.15
)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(G\otimes^{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}h_{X},F)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(h_{X},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(G,F))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PS}}}}(G,F)(X).

This completes the proof of Lemma
1.1
.
∎

Lemma 1.2
.

For
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
and
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, we have a natural isomorphism

\underline{\omega}^{*}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),F)\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),\underline{\omega}^{*}F).

Proof.

For
\mathcal{Y}=(Y,E)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
with
V=Y-|E|
, we have natural isomorphisms

\underline{\omega}^{*}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),F)(\mathcal{Y})\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),F)(V)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X\times V,F)\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset)\otimes\mathcal{Y},\underline{\omega}^{*}F)\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),\underline{\omega}^{*}F)(\mathcal{Y}).

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 1.3
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
and
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, we have a natural isomorphism

\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),F)\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),\underline{\omega}_{!}F).

Proof.

For
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, we have natural isomorphisms

\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),F)(Y)\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),F)(Y,\emptyset)\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X\times Y,\emptyset),F)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X\times Y,\underline{\omega}_{!}F)\\
\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),\underline{\omega}_{!}F)(Y).

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 1.4
.

A complex in
C^{\bullet}
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
such that
C^{n}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
is exact if and only if
C^{\bullet}(K)
is exact as a complex of abelian groups for any function field
K
.

Proof.

The cohomology sheaves
H^{n}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(C^{\bullet})
are in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
by
[
13
, Th.0.1]
.
Hence for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, by
[
13
, Th. 0.2]
there is an injective map
(H^{n}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C^{\bullet})(X)\hookrightarrow(H^{n}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C^{\bullet})(k(X))
. Hence the lemma follows from the fact that

(H^{n}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C^{\bullet})(k(X))\cong H^{n}(C^{\bullet}(k(X)))
, since
k(X)
is henselian local.
∎

Lemma 1.5
.

For
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}
and
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
such that
F
is a quotient of a representable sheaf,
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}
.

Proof.

First assume
F=\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X)
with
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.
Put
\widetilde{G}=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
(cf. (
18
)).
The adjunction (
1.12
) implies
\underline{\omega}_{!}\widetilde{G}\simeq G
.
Lemma
1.3
implies a natural isomorphism

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),G)\simeq\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}).

Thus it suffices to show

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}.

The
{\overline{\square}}
-invariance follows directly from the one for
\widetilde{G}
.
The fact that it is in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}^{\tau}
follows from
[
13
, Lemma 1.27(2)]
.

Now assume there is a surjection
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X)\to F
in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
,
where
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
. It induces an injection

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),G).

Since
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),G)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}
as shown above and

\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}\subset{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
is closed under finite products and subobjects,
we get
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}
as desired. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 1.6
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
be such that
F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
(cf. (
16
)).
For any function field
K
over
k
, we have

H^{i}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},F_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)})=0\text{ for }\;\;i>0.

Proof.

If
F
is semi-pure, the assertion follows from
[
13
, Th. 9.1]
.
In general we use the exact sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
:

0\to C\to F\to F^{sp}\to 0

to reduce to the above case noting
H^{i}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},C_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)})=0
for
i>0
since

C_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)}
is supported on
\{0,\infty\}
.
∎

Lemma 1.7
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
and a function field
K
over
k
, we have

\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F(K)\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F({\overline{\square}}\otimes K).

Proof.

We consider the exact sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
:

0\to C\to F\to F^{sp}\to 0\;\text{ with}\;\;\underline{\omega}_{!}C=0.

Since
\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
is exact, from this we get an exact sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
:

0\to\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C\to\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\to\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F^{sp}\to 0.

Since
C_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)}
is supported on
\{0_{K},\infty_{K}\}
,
we have by (
1.4
)

(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C)_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)}=C_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)}.

Hence the exact sequence gives rise to a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{F(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{sp}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C({\overline{\square}}\otimes K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F({\overline{\square}}\otimes K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F^{sp}({\overline{\square}}\otimes K)}

The left (resp. right ) vertical map is an isomorphism since
C\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
(resp.
thanks to
[
13
, Th. 10.1]
).
This completes the proof.
∎

Let
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}=\operatorname{Spec}k[t]
be the affine line with the coordinate
t
.
Consider the map in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
:

\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}:\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}-\{0\})\to\mathbf{G}_{m}

given by
t\in\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}-\{0\})=k[t,t^{-1}]^{\times}
, and the map in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
:

\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}:\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})\to\mathbf{G}_{a}

given by
t\in\mathbf{G}_{a}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})=k[t]
.
Note that
\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
and
\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}
factor through

\operatorname{Coker}(\mathbb{Z}\overset{i_{1}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}-\{0\}))\quad\text{ and }\quad\operatorname{Coker}(\mathbb{Z}\overset{i_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})),

with
i_{1}
and
i_{0}
induced by the points
1\in\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}-\{0\}
and
0\in\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}
respectively.

Lemma 1.8
.

(1)

The composite map

\omega_{!}\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty)\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}-\{0\})\overset{\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{G}_{m}

induces an isomorphism

(1.16)

a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{m}})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{G}_{m},

where

{\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}=\operatorname{Coker}(\mathbb{Z}\overset{i_{1}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty))\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
.

(2)

The composite map

\omega_{!}\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{P}^{1},2\infty)\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})\overset{\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{G}_{a}

induces an isomorphism

(1.17)

a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{G}_{a},

where

{\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}=\operatorname{Coker}(\mathbb{Z}\overset{i_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{P}^{1},2\infty))\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
.

Proof.

We prove only (2). The proof of (1) is similar.
By
[
13
, Lem. 1.36 and Th. 0.1]
, we have

a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
.
Hence, by Lemma
1.4
, it suffices to show that
the map
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1})(K)\overset{\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{G}_{a}(K)=K
for a function field
K
over
k
, induces an isomorphism

\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}})(K)\simeq K
.
We know that
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})(K)
is identified with the group of
0
-cycles on
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K}=\mathbf{A}^{1}\otimes_{k}K
. Then, by
[
6
, Th. 3.2.1]
, the kernel of

\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1})(K)\to\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}})(K)
is generated by
the class of
0\in\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K}
and

\operatorname{div}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K}}(f)
for
f\in K(t)^{\times}
such that

f\in 1+\mathfrak{m}_{\infty}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},\infty}
, where
\mathfrak{m}_{\infty}
is the maximal ideal of the local ring
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},\infty}
of
\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K}
at
\infty
.
Now (2) follows by an elementary computation.
∎

Lemma 1.9
.

We have

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(G,F)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}\text{ for }\;\;G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}},\;F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}.

Proof.

Put
H=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(G,F)
.
Let
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
and

\textstyle{\mathcal{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}}

be a
\underline{\operatorname{MV}}^{{\operatorname{fin}}}
-square as defined in
[
3
, Def. 3.2.1]
.
By
[
3
, Def. 4.5.2 and Lem. 4.2.3]
, it suffices to show the exactness of

0\to H(\mathcal{X})\to H(\mathcal{U})\oplus H(\mathcal{V})\to H(\mathcal{W}).

By the adjunction, we have

H(\mathcal{X})=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(G,F^{\mathcal{X}})\;\text{ with}\;F^{\mathcal{X}}=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{X}),F)).

Hence it suffices to show the exactness of the following sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
:

0\to F^{\mathcal{X}}\to F^{\mathcal{U}}\oplus F^{\mathcal{V}}\to F^{\mathcal{W}}.

Taking
\mathcal{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
, this is reduced to showing the exactness of

0\to F(\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\to F(\mathcal{U}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\oplus F(\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\to F(\mathcal{W}\otimes\mathcal{Y}).

This follows from the fact that
\underline{\operatorname{MV}}^{{\operatorname{fin}}}
-squares are preserved by the product
\otimes
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
.
∎

Proposition 1.10
.

(i)

For
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
, we have a natural isomorphism

\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}G)\simeq\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G)

induced by the natural maps
F\to\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F
and
G\to\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G
.

(ii)

For
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
, we have a natural isomorphism

a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\simeq a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G)

induced by the natural maps
F\to a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F
and
G\to a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G
.

Proof.

For
H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
, we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}G),H)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}G,H)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(F,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(G,H))

\displaystyle\overset{(*1)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(G,H))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}G,H)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(G,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F,H))

\displaystyle\overset{(*2)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F,H))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G,H)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G),H)

where
(*1)
and
(*2)
follow from the fact

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(A,H)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
for
A\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
by Lemma
1.9
.
This proves (i).

For
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
, we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\tau_{!}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*1)}{\simeq}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\tau_{!}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*2)}{\simeq}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\tau_{!}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\tau_{!}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*3)}{\simeq}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\tau_{!}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\tau_{!}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*4)}{\simeq}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\tau_{!}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\tau_{!}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*5)}{\simeq}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\tau_{!}(a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*6)}{\simeq}\tau_{!}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G)

where
(*1)
,
(*4)
and
(*6)
follow from (
1.3
),
(*2)
and
(*5)

follow from the monoidality of
\tau_{!}
(
[
10
, §3.8]
) and
(*3)
follows from (i). Since
\tau_{!}
is fully faithful, this implies (ii).
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 1.11
.

There are natural isomorphisms for
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}

(1.18)

(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)^{sp}\simeq(F^{sp}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)^{sp}\simeq(F^{sp}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G^{sp})^{sp}.

Proof.

We have an exact sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
:

0\to C\to F\to F^{sp}\to 0\;\text{ with}\;\;\omega_{!}C=0.

Since
(-)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G:\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
is right exact, we get an exact sequence

C\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G\to F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G\to F^{sp}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G\to 0.

We have
\omega_{!}(C\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)=0
since
\omega_{!}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}\to{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}

is monoidal by
[
10
, §3.6]
. Hence we get an isomorphism

(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)^{sp}\simeq(F^{sp}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)^{sp}
.
This implies (
1.18
).
∎

Lemma 1.12
.

There are natural isomorphisms for
F,G,H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}

(1.19)

h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(F^{sp})^{sp}\simeq h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(F)^{sp},

(1.20)

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\simeq h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(G)).

Proof.

We have an exact sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
:

0\to C\to F\to F^{sp}\to 0\;\text{ with}\;\;\omega_{!}C=0.

From this we get an exact sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
:

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(C)\to h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F)\to h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F^{sp})\to 0

since
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
is right exact.
We have
\omega_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(C)=0
since
\omega_{!}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}\to{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}

is exact and
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(C)
is a quotient of
C
. Hence we get an isomorphism

\omega_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F)\simeq\omega_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F^{sp})
.
This implies (
1.19
).

For
H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
, we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G),H)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G,H)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(F,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(G,H))

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F),\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(G,H))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G,H))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G),H))

where
(*)
follows from the fact that
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(G,H)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
for
H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
,
which follows easily from the definition. This shows

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\simeq h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G),

which implies (
1.20
).
∎

From (
1.9
), we have
\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp})\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, which implies

a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp})\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}.

Indeed, for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}
, we have
\tau_{!}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\simeq\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\tau_{!}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}

by (
1.3
), which implies
a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
by definition (cf. (
11
) and
[
4
, Def. 3]
).
Thus we get an induced functor

(1.21)

a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}:\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}.

By definition we have

(1.22)

a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}(F)=a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}j(F)\text{ for }\;\;F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp},

where
j:\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
is the inclusion.

Lemma 1.13
.

a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}
is a left adjoint to the inclusion
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}
.

Proof.

This follows easily from the fact that
a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
is a left adjoint to the inclusion
\operatorname{\mathbf{MNST}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
and the inclusions
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
and

\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MNST}}
are fully faithful.
∎

Lemma 1.14
.

Consider the functors

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}\;:\;F\to h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F)^{sp},

h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\;:\;F\to a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F).

(i)

The functor
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}
(resp.
h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}
) is a left adjoint to the inclusion

\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
).
For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
, we have natural isomorphisms

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\simeq h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\quad\text{ and }\quad h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\simeq h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F).

(ii)

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
, the natural map
F\to a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F
induces isomorphisms

h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\simeq h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F).

(iii)

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
, we have natural isomorphisms

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\simeq h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(G)),

h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\simeq h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(G)).

Proof.

The first statement of (i) follows from the left-adjointness of
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}
,
(-)^{sp}
and
a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
The second statement of (i) is a formal consequence of the first since the inclusions are fully faithful.

To show (ii), consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}}
\scriptstyle{j_{\operatorname{Nis}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\mathbf{MNST}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}

where the functors are inclusions.
For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
and
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}(h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}ia_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F,G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*1)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}ia_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F,i_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*2)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(ia_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F,ji_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(ia_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F,ij_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*3)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MNST}}}(a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F,j_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(F,ij_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(F,ji_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*4)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}F,i_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*5)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}F,G)

where
(*1)
and
(*5)
(resp.
(*2)
and
(*4)
, resp.
(*3)
) follow from Lemma
1.13
(resp. (i), resp. the full faithfulness of
i
). This proves (ii).

For
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
, we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{h0cube2}}{\simeq}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}((F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)^{sp})^{sp}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{MPSTsp1}}{\simeq}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}((F^{sp}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G^{sp})^{sp})^{sp}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{h0cube2}}{\simeq}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F^{sp}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G^{sp}))^{sp}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{h0cube3}}{\simeq}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F^{sp})\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(G^{sp}))^{sp}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{h0cube2}}{\simeq}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}((h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F^{sp})\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(G^{sp}))^{sp})^{sp}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{MPSTsp1}}{\simeq}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}((h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F^{sp})^{sp}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(G^{sp})^{sp})^{sp})^{sp}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{h0cube2}}{\simeq}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}((h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(G))^{sp})^{sp}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{h0cube2}}{\simeq}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(G))^{sp}

\displaystyle=h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(G))

This proves the first isomorphism of (iii). From this we get natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)

\displaystyle\simeq h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(G))

\displaystyle\overset{(*1)}{\simeq}h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(G))

\displaystyle\overset{(*2)}{\simeq}h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}a_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(G))

\displaystyle\overset{(*3)}{\simeq}h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(G))

where
(*1)
and
(*3)
follow from (ii) and
(*2)
follows from Proposition
1.10
in view of (
1.22
).
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

2.
Some lemmas on contractions

For an integer
a\geq 1
put
{\overline{\square}}^{(a)}=(\mathbf{P}^{1},a(0+\infty))\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
and

{\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}=\operatorname{Ker}\big(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)})\to\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\operatorname{Spec}k,\emptyset)\big).

The inclusion
\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}
induces a map
{\overline{\square}}^{(a)}\to{\overline{\square}}
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
for all
a
.
Note that the composite map

(2.1)

{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\hookrightarrow{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\to{\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}

is an isomorphism, where
{\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
is from (
1.16
).

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
, we write

\gamma F=\operatorname{Coker}\big(\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}},F)\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)},F)\big)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}},

where the map is induced by
{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\to{\overline{\square}}
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
.
If
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
, the projection
{\overline{\square}}\to\operatorname{Spec}k
induces an isomorphism

F=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\operatorname{Spec}k,F)\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}},F).

Thus we get an isomorphism

(2.2)

\gamma F\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},F)\overset{(*)}{=}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}),F)\text{ for }\;\;F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau},

where the equality
(*)
follows from the adjunction from (
15
). Note
\gamma F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
.
We also define

\gamma_{\operatorname{Nis}}F=\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\gamma F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}.

By (
2.2
) and Lemma
1.9
, we have

\gamma_{\operatorname{Nis}}F=\gamma F\text{ for }\;\;F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{\operatorname{Nis}},

We write for an integer
n\geq 1
(cf, §1(
19
))

(2.3)

\gamma^{n}F\cong\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}n},F)\cong\overbrace{\gamma\gamma\cdots\gamma}^{n\;\text{times}}F.

Notice that it is

The proof of the following Lemma is due to Kay Rülling. We thank him for letting us include it in our paper.

Lemma 2.1
.

The unit map

(2.4)

\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})^{sp}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\underline{\omega}^{*}\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})\cong\underline{\omega}^{*}(\mathbf{G}_{m}\oplus\mathbb{Z})

is an isomorphism, where the second isomorphism in (
2.4
) holds by
Lemma
1.8
and (
2.1
).

Proof.

(Kay Rülling)

The unit map is injective by semipurity.
It remains to show the surjectivity.
By definition of the sheafification functor, it suffices to show the surjectivity on
(\operatorname{Spec}R,(f))
, where
R
is an integral local
k
-algebra and
f\in R\setminus\{0\}
, such that
R_{f}
is regular.
Denote by

\psi:\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty)(R,f)\to R_{f}^{\times}\oplus\mathbb{Z}

the precomposition of
(
2.4
)
evaluated at
(R,f)
with the
quotient map
\mathbb{Z}_{tr}(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty)(R,f)\to\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})^{sp}
.

We show that
\psi
is surjective. To this end, observe that for
a\in R_{f}^{\times}
we find
N\geq 0
and
b\in R
such that

(2.5)

ab=f^{N},\quad\text{and}\quad af^{N}\in R.

Set
W:=V(t^{N}-a)\subset\operatorname{Spec}R_{f}[t,1/t]
and
K:=\text{Frac}(R)
.

The map
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(K,\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\})\to\operatorname{Pic}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)\cong K^{\times}\oplus\mathbb{Z}
which induces the second isomorphism of (
2.4
) sends a prime correspondence
V(a_{0}+a_{1}t+\ldots a_{r}t^{r})
to
((-1)^{r}a_{0}/a_{r},r)
, hence we have:

(2.6)

\psi(V(a_{0}+a_{1}t+\ldots a_{r}t^{r}))=((-1)^{r}a_{0}/a_{r},r)

provided that
V(a_{0}+a_{1}t+\ldots a_{r}t^{r})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((R,f),(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty))
.

For any
a\in R_{f}^{\times}
, consider
h=t^{N}-a
and let
h=\prod_{i}h_{i}
be the decomposition into monic irreducible factors in
K[t,1/t]

and denote by
W_{i}\subset\operatorname{Spec}R_{f}[t,1/t]
the closure of
V(h_{i})
. (Note that
W_{i}=W_{j}
for
i\neq j
is allowed.)

The
W_{i}
correspond to the components of
W
which are dominant over
R_{f}
; since
W
is finite and surjective over
R_{f}
, so are the
W_{i}
. We claim

(2.7)

W_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}((R,f),(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty))

Indeed, let
I_{i}
(resp.
J_{i}
) be the ideal of the closure of
W_{i}
in
\operatorname{Spec}R[t]
(resp.
\operatorname{Spec}R[z]
with
z=1/t
).
By (
2.5
)

bt^{N}-f^{N}\in I_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad f^{N}-f^{N}az^{N}\in J_{i}.

Hence
(f/t)^{N}\in R[t]/I_{i}
and
(f/z)^{N}\in R[z]/J_{i}
.
It follows that
f/t
(resp.
f/z
) is integral over
R[t]/I_{i}
(resp.
R[z]/J_{i}
);
thus (
2.7
) holds.
We claim

\psi(\sum_{i}W_{i})=((-1)^{N+1}a,N).

Indeed, it suffices to show this after restriction to the generic point of
R
,
in which case it follows directly from the definition of the
W_{i}
and (
2.6
).
Since
\psi(V(t\pm 1))=(-(\pm 1),1)
, this implies the surjectivity of
\psi
and proves the lemma.

∎

Corollary 2.2
.

(1)

There is a natural isomorphism

\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})^{sp}\simeq\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}.

(2)

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
,
we have a natural isomorphism

(2.8)

\gamma F\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},F).

Proof.

(1) is a direct consequence of Lemma
2.1
.
In view of (
2.2
), (2) follows from (1) and the adjunctin of
