\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
that from §1(
14
).
∎

Lemma 2.3
.

Consider an exact sequence
0\to A\to B\to C\to 0
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
.

(1)

Assume
A,B,C\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. Then the following sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}

0\to\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma A\to\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma B\to\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma C\to 0

is exact.

(2)

Assume
\underline{\omega}_{!}A=0
and
C\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. Then the following sequence

0\to\gamma A(K)\to\gamma B(K)\to\gamma C(K)\to 0

is exact for any function field
K
over
k
.

Proof.

First assume
A,B,C\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. Then all terms of the sequence in (1) are in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
.
By Lemma
1.4
, it suffices to show the exactness of

0\to\gamma A(K)\to\gamma B(K)\to\gamma C(K)\to 0

for a function field
K
over
k
.

By (
2.2
), we have
\gamma F(K)=\operatorname{Hom}({{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}_{,K},F)
for all
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
where
{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}_{,K}={\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\operatorname{Spec}K
.
Since
{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}_{,K}
is a direct summand of
\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)
, it is enough to show that

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty),A)=0.

By using
[
3
, Th.2(2)]
we can compute

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty),A)\simeq H^{1}_{\operatorname{Nis}}(\mathbf{P}_{K}^{1},A_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)}),

where we used the fact that any proper birational map
X\to\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K}
is an isomorphism.
Thus the vanishing follows from Lemma
1.6
. This proves (1).

Next we assume
\underline{\omega}_{!}A=0
and
C\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
For a function field
K
over
k
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},\infty)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},\infty)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},\infty)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

where the sequences are exact since for every effective Cartier divisor
D
on
\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K}
,

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},D),A)\simeq H^{1}_{\operatorname{Nis}}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},A_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},D)})=0,

by
[
3
, Th.2(2)]
and the fact that
A_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},D)}
is supported on the zero-dimensional scheme
|D|
by the assumption. Finally,
\operatorname{Ker}(c)=0
by
[
13
, Th. 3.1]
, hence the snake lemma gives the exact sequence of (2).
∎

Proposition 2.4
.

(1)

Take
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
(cf. §1(
16
)). For
\mathcal{X}=(X,D_{X})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}_{ls}
(cf. §1(
3
)), there exists a map functorial in
\mathcal{X}
:

(2.9)

\gamma F(\mathcal{X})\to H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X,F_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}}).

Moreover, if
X
is henselian local, it is an isomorphism.

(2)

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
be such that
F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
For
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, there exists a map functorial in
X
:

(2.10)

\gamma F(X)\to H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X,F_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X}).

Moreover, it is an isomorphism either if
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
and
X
is henselian local,
or if
X=\operatorname{Spec}(K)
is the spectrum of a function field over
k
and the natural map
F(K)\to F({\overline{\square}}\otimes K)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Let
L=(\mathbf{P}^{1},0)
. We prove (1).
By (
2.2
) and
[
13
, Lem. 7.1]
, there exists an exact sequence of sheaves on
(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X)_{\operatorname{Nis}}
:

(2.11)

0\to F_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}}\to F_{L\otimes\mathcal{X}}\to i_{*}\gamma F_{\mathcal{X}}\to 0,

where
i:X\to\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X
is induced by
0\in\mathbf{P}^{1}
.
Taking cohomology, we get the map (
2.9
).
If
X
is henselian local, we have

(2.12)

H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X,F_{L\otimes\mathcal{X}})\simeq H^{1}(X,F_{\mathcal{X}})=0

thanks to
[
13
, Th. 9.3]
.
Note that the map
F(\mathcal{X})\to F(L\otimes\mathcal{X})
induced by the projection

L\otimes\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}
is an isomorphism by the
{\overline{\square}}
-invariance of
F
.
Since the projection factors as
L\otimes\mathcal{X}\to\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}
, this implies
the map
F(\mathbf{P}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{X})\to F(L\otimes\mathcal{X})
is surjective. This implies that the map (
2.9
) is an isomorphism.

We prove (2).
Consider the exact sequence of sheaves on
(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X)_{\operatorname{Nis}}
:

(2.13)

0\to F_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X}\to F_{L\otimes X}\to i_{*}\lambda_{X}F\to 0,

where
\lambda_{X}F=i^{*}(F_{L\otimes X}/F_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X})
.
The injectivity of the first map follows from
[
13
, Th. 3.1]
noting

F_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X}=F^{sp}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X}

1
1

1

The point is that
X
has the empty modulus.

and
F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
by the assumption.
Taking cohomology over an étale
U\to X
, we get a map natural in
U
:

\lambda_{X}F(U)\to H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times U,F_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\times U}).

To define the map (
2.10
), it suffices to show the following.

Claim 2.5
.

There exists a natural map of sheaves on
X_{\operatorname{Nis}}
:

\varphi_{F,X}:(\gamma_{\operatorname{Nis}}F)_{X}\to\lambda_{X}F.

It is an isomorphism if
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
.
If
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
and
F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{\operatorname{Nis}}

k
.

By definition,
\lambda_{X}F
is the sheaf on

Since the colimit in (

\textstyle{(\gamma F^{sp})_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\lambda_{X}F^{sp}}

The upper sequence is exact by Lemma

Since we know that

C_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times U,0+\infty)}
(resp.
C_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times U,\infty)}
) is supported on
\{0_{U},\infty_{U}\}
(resp.
\infty_{U}
).
This proves that
\varphi_{C,X}
is an isomorphism and completes the proof of the claim.

To show the second assertion of (2), we look at the cohomology exact sequence arising from (
2.13
). Note that

F(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X)\to F(L\otimes X)
is surjective since

F(X)\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}F(L\otimes X)
by the assumption.
Hence it suffices to show

H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X,F_{L\otimes X})=0
. If
F
is semi-pure, this follows from (
2.12
).
In general it is reduced to the above case using (
2.15
) and noting

H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}\times X,C_{L\otimes X})=0
since
C_{L\otimes X}
is supported on
0\times X
.
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Corollary 2.6
.

Let
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
and
K
be a function field
K
over
k
.

(1)

There is a natural isomorphism

\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G(K)\simeq H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G).

(2)

The natural map

\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G(K)\to\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G^{sp}(K)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Letting
F=\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G
, we have
F^{sp}=\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
by
§1(
1.9
).
By Lemma
1.7
,
F
satisfies the second assumption of Proposition
2.4
(2).
Hence (1) follows from Proposition
2.4
(2).
(2) follows from isomorphisms

\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G(K)\simeq H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G)\simeq H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G)\simeq H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\underline{\omega}_{!}G)\\
\simeq H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\underline{\omega}_{!}G^{sp})\simeq H^{1}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G^{sp})\simeq\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G^{sp}(K),

where the third (resp. last) isomorphism follows from §1(
13
) (resp. Proposition
2.4
).
∎

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
.

(1)

The natural map

\gamma F(K)\to\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F(K)

is an isomorphism for any function field
K
over
k
.

(2)

The natural map

\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\gamma F^{sp}\to\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F^{sp}

is injective.

(3)

The natural map

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\gamma F^{sp}\to\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F^{sp}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider the exact sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
:

(2.16)

0\to C\to F\to F^{sp}\to 0\;\text{ with}\;\;\underline{\omega}_{!}C=0.

By §1(
1.7
), we have
C,F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
.
It gives rise to an exact sequence in
\operatorname{\mathbf{{\underline{M}}NST}}
:

0\to\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C\to\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\to\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F^{sp}\to 0

and a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\gamma C(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\gamma F(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\gamma F^{sp}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F^{sp}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

The upper sequence is exact thanks to (
2.2
).
The lower sequence is exact by Lemma
2.3
(2) noting
\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
by
[
13
, Th. 10.1]
and

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C=a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\underline{\omega}_{!}C=0
(cf. §1(
13
)).
Since
C_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)}
is supported on
\{0_{K},\infty_{K}\}
,
we have by §1(
1.4
)

(\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C)_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)}=C_{(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},0+\infty)},

where we used the fact that any proper birational map between normal schemes of dimension
1
is an isomorphism.
Hence the left vertical map is an isomorphism. Hence we may assume that

F
is semi-pure.
By §1(
1.9
),
we have
\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
.
By
[
13
, Lem. 5.9]
, we have natural isomorphisms

\gamma F(K)\simeq F(\mathbf{A}_{K}^{1},0)/F(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K},\emptyset),

\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F(K)\simeq\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F(\mathbf{A}_{K}^{1},0)/\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{K},\emptyset).

Hence (1) follows from
[
13
, Th. 4.1]
.

To show (2) and (3), first note that
F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
by the assumption and §1(
1.7
) and hence
\gamma F^{sp}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
.
By §1(
1.9
),
\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\gamma F^{sp}
and
\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F^{sp}
are in
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, and hence
\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\gamma F^{sp}
and
\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F^{sp}
are in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
.
Hence (2) (resp. (3)) follows from (1) for
F=F^{sp}
and
[
13
, Cor. 3.4]

(resp. Lemma
1.4
).

∎

Lemma 2.8
.

Consider a sequence
A\to B\to C
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
such that

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}A\to\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}B\to\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C\to 0

is exact in
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
. Then the following sequence

\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}A(K)\to\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}B(K)\to\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}C(K)\to 0

is exact for any function field
K
over
k
.

Proof.

In view of the right exactness of the functor

H^{1}(\mathbf{P}_{K},-):\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Ab}},

the lemma follows from Corollary
2.6
(1) by applying the above functor to the first exact sequence.
∎

Corollary 2.9
.

Let
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
. Then for any function field
K
we have an isomorphism
\gamma F(K)\cong\gamma\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F(K)

Proof.

Let
q:\gamma(F)(K)\to\gamma(\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F)(K)
be the map induced by the unit map
F\hookrightarrow\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F
for the adjunction (
1.13
), which is injective since it factors the map
F\hookrightarrow\underline{\omega}^{*}\underline{\omega}_{!}F
. Notice that
q
is injective by (
2.2
) and the fact that
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}_{,K},\_)
preserves injective maps, hence it is enough to show that it is surjective. Let
Q
be the presheaf cokernel of
F\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\omega_{!}F
, hence
Q\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
and
\underline{\omega}_{!}Q=0
. By Lemma
2.8
we have an exact sequence

\gamma F(K)\xrightarrow{q}\gamma\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\omega_{!}F(K)\to\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}Q(K)\to 0.

By Corollary
2.6
(2) we have that

\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}Q(K)\cong\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}Q^{sp}(K)=0,

hence
q
is surjective.
∎

Proposition 2.10
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, there is a natural isomorphism

\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma F\simeq\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},F)\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}_{!}F).

Proof.

The first isomorphism follows from (
2.2
) and Corollary
2.2
.
For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
and
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, put

F^{X}=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset)),F).

Note that
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
implies
F^{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
.
We compute

\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma F(X)=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},F)(X,\emptyset)\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},F^{X})=\gamma F^{X}(k),

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}_{!}F)(X)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,\underline{\omega}_{!}F))\\
\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}_{!}F^{X})(k),

where the last isomorphism comes from Lemma
1.3
.
Hence it suffices to show that there exists a natural isomorphism for any
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
:

\gamma F(k)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}_{!}F).

We have isomorphisms

\begin{aligned} \operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}_{!}F)&\overset{(*1)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{*}\underline{\omega}_{!}F)\\
&\overset{(*2)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F)\\
&\overset{(*3)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F)\\
&\overset{(*4)}{\simeq}\gamma\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F(k)\overset{(*5)}{\simeq}\gamma F(k),\end{aligned}

where
(*1)
follows from the fact that
\underline{\omega}^{*}
is fully faithful (cf. §1(
8
)), and

(*2)
from the adjunction from §1(
15
) (see also (
1.12
)) in view of the fact
\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
by Lemma
2.1
,
(*3)
from Lemma
2.1
,
(*4)
by (
2.2
) and
(*5)
by Corollary
2.9
,

∎

3.
Weak cancellation theorem

For
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
we write (cf. §1(
16
), (
19
) and Lemma
1.14
)

F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G=h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}},

F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}G=h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp},

F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}G=h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}.

Proposition 3.1
.

The product
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}
(resp.
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}
, resp.
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}
) defines a symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}
, resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
).

Proof.

The assertion follows immediately from the fact that
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}
defines a symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
except the associativity.
We prove it only for
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}
(other cases are similar).
We need to show a natural isomorphism for
F,G,H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
:

(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}G)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}H\simeq F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}(G\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}H).

For simplicity we write
\lambda=h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}
.
For
F,G,H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
, we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\lambda(\lambda(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}H)

\displaystyle\overset{(*1)}{\simeq}\lambda(\lambda^{2}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}\lambda H)

\displaystyle\overset{(*2)}{\simeq}\lambda(\lambda(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}\lambda H)

\displaystyle\overset{(*3)}{\simeq}\lambda((F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}G)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}H)

where
(*1)
(resp.
(*2)
, resp.
(*3)
) follows from Lemma
1.14

(iii)
(resp.
(i)
, resp.
(iii)
).
The lemma follows from this and the associativity of
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}
.
∎

For
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
we write

F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G=\tau_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\tau^{*}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}\tau^{*}G)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau},

F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}G=\tau_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(\tau^{*}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}\tau^{*}G)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp},

F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}G=\tau_{!}h_{0,{\operatorname{Nis}}}^{{\overline{\square}},sp}(\tau^{*}F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}\tau^{*}G)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}.

By §1(
1.3
), we have a natural isomorphism

(3.1)

\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}G)\simeq F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}G.

In view of the equivalences (
1.8
) and (
1.10
),
Proposition
3.1
implies

Proposition 3.2
.

The product
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}
(resp.
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}
, resp.
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}
) defines a symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
(resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
, resp.
\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
).
There is a natural isomorphism for
F,G,H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}

(3.2)

(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}G)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}H\simeq F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}(G\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}H).

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
and an integer
d\geq 0
, we put

(3.3)

F(d)=({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}d}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}F.

Note
F(d)=F(m)(n)
with
d=m+n
by (
3.2
).

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
and
f\in F(\mathcal{X})
with
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
, consider the composite map

{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{X})\overset{id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\otimes f}{\longrightarrow}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}F\to{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F.

By the adjunction
({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}-)\dashv\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},-)

this gives rise to a natural map

(3.4)

\iota_{F}:F\to\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F),

which induces

(3.5)

\iota_{F}^{sp}:F^{sp}\to\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}F),

noting the adjunction from §1(
14
) and the fact that
\gamma:\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}\to\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
preserves semipure objects.

If
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, this induces a natural map

(3.6)

\iota_{F}:F^{sp}\to\gamma(F(1)).

which generalizes to a natural map for
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}

(cf. (
3.3
) and (
2.3
))

(3.7)

\iota^{n}_{F}:F^{sp}\to\gamma^{n}(F(n)),

noting

\gamma^{n}F=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}n},F)\text{ for }\;\;F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}

thanks to the adjunction from (
15
).

Question 3.3
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, is the map (
3.6
) an isomorphism?

We will prove the following variant.

Theorem 3.4
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
, the map (
3.5
) is an isomorphism.

Before going into its proof, we give some consequences.

Corollary 3.5
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
the map (
3.5
) gives an isomorphism

\underline{\omega}_{!}\iota_{F}:\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}F).

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
,
the map (
3.7
) induces an isomorphism

\underline{\omega}_{!}\iota^{n}_{F}:\underline{\omega}_{!}F\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{n}F(n).

Proof.

The functors
\underline{\omega}_{!}
and
\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
are exact and
\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G\cong\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}G^{sp}
for all
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
.
Hence Theorem
3.4
gives a natural isomorphism

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\iota_{F}:\omega_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}F\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}F).

This proves the first assertion since Lemma
2.7
(3) implies

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}F)\simeq\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}F).

The second assertion for the case
n=1
follows directly from the first.
For
n>1
, we proceed by the induction on
n
to assume

(3.8)

\underline{\omega}_{!}\iota^{n-1}_{F}:\underline{\omega}_{!}F\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{n-1}F(n-1).

Then we have isomorphisms

\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{n}F(n)\overset{(*1)}{\simeq}\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma\gamma^{n-1}F(n)\overset{(*2)}{\simeq}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{n-1}F(n))=\\
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{n-1}F(1)(n-1))\overset{(*3)}{\simeq}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}_{!}F(1))\\
\overset{(*4)}{\simeq}\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma F(1)\overset{(*5)}{\simeq}\underline{\omega}_{!}F,

where
(*1)
follows from
(
2.3
),
(*2)
follows from Proposition
2.10
noting

\gamma^{n-1}F(n)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
,
(*3)
follows from (
3.8
),
(*4)
follows from Proposition
2.10
and
(*5)
follows from the case
n=1
.
This completes the proof.∎

Corollary 3.6
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
and
\widetilde{F}=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
(cf. (
1.13
)),
the map (
3.7
)
\iota^{n}_{\widetilde{F}}:\widetilde{F}\to\gamma^{n}\widetilde{F}(n)
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{n}_{\widetilde{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\gamma^{n}\widetilde{F}(n)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hookrightarrow}
\textstyle{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\widetilde{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\iota_{\widetilde{F}}}
\textstyle{\enskip\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{n}\widetilde{F}(n)}

where the vertical arrows come from the adjunction (
1.13
).
The left (resp. right) vertical arrow is an isomorphism (resp. injective) since

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\simeq id
(resp. by the semipurity of
\gamma^{n}\widetilde{F}(n)
).
Since
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\iota^{n}_{\widetilde{F}}
is an isomorphism by
Corollary
3.5
, this implies
\iota^{n}_{\widetilde{F}}
is an isomorphism by Snake Lemma.
∎

Corollary 3.7
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, there is a natural injective map

\tilde{\rho}_{F}:\gamma^{n}F(n)\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F

whose composite with the map (
3.7
)
\iota^{n}_{F}:F\to\gamma^{n}F(n)
coincides with the unit map
u:F\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\omega_{!}F
for the adjunction (
1.13
).
In particular (
3.7
) is injective.

Proof.

Define
\tilde{\rho}_{F}
as the composite

\gamma^{n}F(n)\overset{u}{\longrightarrow}\gamma^{n}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F(n)\overset{(\iota^{n}_{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\omega_{!}F})^{-1}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\omega_{!}F,

where the second map is the inverse of the isomorphism

\iota^{n}_{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\omega_{!}F}:\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\omega_{!}F\cong\gamma^{n}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\omega_{!}F(n)
from Corollary
3.6
.
Clearly we have
\tilde{\rho}_{F}\circ\iota^{n}_{F}=u
. We easily see that

\tilde{\rho}_{F}
coincides with the composite

\gamma^{n}F(n)\overset{u}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{n}F(n)\overset{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}(\underline{\omega}_{!}\iota^{n}_{F})^{-1}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}F,

where the first map is injective by the semipurity of
\gamma^{n}F(n)
and
the second map is induced by the inverse of the isomorphism

\underline{\omega}_{!}\iota^{n}_{F}:\underline{\omega}_{!}F\cong\underline{\omega}_{!}\gamma^{n}F(n)
from
Corollary
3.5
.
This completes the proof.
∎

In the rest of this section we prove the following.

Proposition 3.8
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
, the map (
3.5
)
\iota_{F}^{sp}
is split injective.

For the proof of Proposition
3.8
we first recall the construction of
[
15
]
.
Take
X,Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.
For an integer
n>0
consider the rational function on
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}
:

g_{n}=\frac{x_{1}^{n+1}-1}{x_{1}^{n+1}-x_{2}}.

Let
D_{XY}(g_{n})
be the divisor of the pullback of
g_{n}
to

(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}-0)\times X\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}-0)\times Y
.
Take a prime correspondence

(3.9)

Z\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}-0)\times X,(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}-0)\times Y).

Let
{\overline{Z}}\subset\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{1}}\times X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{2}}\times Y
be the closure of
Z
and
{\overline{Z}}^{N}
be its normalization.

Lemma 3.9
.

(1)

Let
N>0
be an integer such that

(3.10)

N(0_{1}+\infty_{1})_{|{\overline{Z}}^{N}}\geq(0_{2}+\infty_{2})_{|{\overline{Z}}^{N}}.

Then, for any integer
n\geq N
,

Z
intersects properly with
|D_{XY}(g_{n})|
and any component of
the intersection
Z\cdot D_{XY}(g_{n})
is finite and surjective over
X
.
Thus we get

\rho_{n}(Z)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X,Y)

as the pushforward of
Z\cdot D_{XY}(g_{n})
in
X\times Y
.

(2)

If
Z=Id_{(\mathbf{A}^{1}-0)}\otimes W
for
W\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X,Y)
, then one can take
N=1
in (1) and
\rho_{n}(Z)=W
.

(3)

For any
Z
as in (
3.9
) such that
\rho_{n}(Z)
is defined and for any
f\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})

with
X^{\prime},Y^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
,
\rho_{n}(Z\otimes f)
for

Z\otimes f\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}-0)\times(X\times X^{\prime}),(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}-0)\times(Y\times Y^{\prime}))

is defined and we have

\rho_{n}(Z\otimes f)=\rho_{n}(Z)\otimes f\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X\times X^{\prime},Y\times Y^{\prime}).

(4)

For an integer
N>0
let

\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}^{(N)}((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}-0)\times X,(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}-0)\times Y)

be the subgroup of
\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}-0)\times X,(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}-0)\times Y))

generated by prime correspondences satisfying
the condition (
3.10
). Then the presheaf on
\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
given by

X\to\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}^{(N)}((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}-0)\times X,(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}-0)\times Y)

is a Nisnevich sheaf.

Proof.

The assertions are proved in
[
15
, Lem. 4.1, 4.3 and 4.5]
except that
(4) follows from the fact that the condition (
3.10
) is Nisnevich local on
X
.
∎

For an integer
a\geq 1
put
{\overline{\square}}^{(a)}=(\mathbf{P}^{1},a(0+\infty))\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
.
Take
\mathcal{X}=({\overline{X}},X_{\infty}),\mathcal{Y}=({\overline{Y}},Y_{\infty})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
with

X={\overline{X}}-|X_{\infty}|
and
Y={\overline{Y}}-|Y_{\infty}|
.
For
a\geq 1
take a prime correspondence

Z\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes\mathcal{Y}).

By definition
Z\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X,Y)

and it satisfies

(3.11)

(0_{2}+\infty_{2})_{|{\overline{Z}}^{N}}+(Y_{\infty})_{|{\overline{Z}}^{N}}\leq a(0_{1}+\infty_{1})_{|{\overline{Z}}^{N}}+(X_{\infty})_{|{\overline{Z}}^{N}},

where
{\overline{Z}}^{N}
is the normalization of the closure
{\overline{Z}}
of
Z
in

\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{1}}\times X\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{2}}\times{\overline{Y}}
.

For integers
n,m\geq a
, we consider the rational function on
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}
:

h=tg_{n}+(1-t)g_{m}.

Let
D_{X\mathbf{A}^{1}Y}(h)
be the divisor of the pullback of
h
to

(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}-0)\times X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}-0)\times Y
.
By
[
15
, Rem. 4.2]
,

Z\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}
intersects properly with
|D_{X\mathbf{A}^{1}Y}(h)|
and any component of the intersection
(Z\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})\cdot D_{X\mathbf{A}^{1}Y}(h)
is finite and surjective over
X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}
.
Thus we get

\rho_{h}(Z\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t},Y).

It is easy to see

(3.12)

i_{0}^{*}\rho_{h}(Z\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})=\rho_{m}(Z)\quad\text{ and }\quad i_{1}^{*}\rho_{h}(Z\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})=\rho_{n}(Z).

Lemma 3.10
.

For
n,m\geq a
,

\rho_{h}(Z\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{Y})
.

Proof.

Let
V
be any component of
(Z\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})\cdot D_{X\mathbf{A}^{1}Y}(h)
and
\overline{V}
be its closure in

\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{1}}\times{\overline{X}}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{2}}\times{\overline{Y}}.

Let
W\subset X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}\times Y
be the image of
V
and

\overline{W}
be its closure in
{\overline{X}}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t}\times{\overline{Y}}
. Then we have

\overline{W}=\pi(\overline{V})
, where

\pi:\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{1}}\times{\overline{X}}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{2}}\times{\overline{Y}}\to{\overline{X}}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t}\times{\overline{Y}}

is the projection. We want to show

(Y_{\infty})_{|\overline{W}^{N}}\leq({\overline{X}}\times\infty)_{|\overline{W}^{N}}+(X_{\infty}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t})_{|\overline{W}^{N}}.

Since
\pi:\overline{V}^{N}\to\overline{W}^{N}
is proper and surjective, this is reduced to showing

(Y_{\infty})_{|\overline{V}^{N}}\leq({\overline{X}}\times\infty)_{|\overline{V}^{N}}+(X_{\infty}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t})_{|\overline{V}^{N}}

by
[
9
, Lem. 2.2]
. By (
3.11
) and the containment lemma
[
9
, Pr. 2.4]
(see also
[
1
, Lem. 2.1]
), we have

(Y_{\infty})_{|\overline{V}^{N}}+(0_{2}+\infty_{2})_{|\overline{V}^{N}}\leq a(0_{1}+\infty_{1})_{|\overline{V}^{N}}+(X_{\infty}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t})_{|\overline{V}^{N}}.

Thus it suffices to show

a(0_{1}+\infty_{1})_{|\overline{V}^{N}}\leq(0_{2}+\infty_{2})_{|\overline{V}^{N}}+\infty_{|\overline{V}^{N}}.

Using
[
9
, Pr. 2.4]
again, this follows from

(3.13)

a(0_{1}+\infty_{1})_{|T}\leq(0_{2}+\infty_{2})_{|T}+\infty_{|T},

where
T\subset\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{1}}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{2}}
is any component of the closure of the divisor of
h
on

(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}-0)\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}-0)
.
By an easy computation,
T
is contained in one of the closures

\overline{D(H)}
,
\overline{D(J_{n})}
,
\overline{D(J_{m})}
of the divisors of

H=t(x_{1}^{n+1}-x_{1}^{m+1})(1-x_{2})+(x_{1}^{m+1}-1)(x_{1}^{n+1}-x_{2}),

J_{n}=x_{1}^{n+1}-x_{2},\quad J_{m}=x_{1}^{m+1}-x_{2}

respectively.
Letting
\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{i}}-0=\operatorname{Spec}k[\tau_{i}]
with
\tau_{i}=x_{i}^{-1}
for
i=1,2
,

\overline{D(H)}
,
\overline{D(J_{n})}
,
\overline{D(J_{m})}
are defined in

(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{1}}-0)\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}\times(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{2}}-0)
by the ideals generated by

H^{\prime}=t(\tau_{1}^{m+1}-\tau_{1}^{n+1})(\tau_{2}-1)+(1-\tau_{1}^{m+1})(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}^{n+1}),

J_{n}^{\prime}=\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}^{n+1},\quad J_{m}^{\prime}=\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}^{m+1}.

Hence,
\overline{D(H)}
,
\overline{D(J_{n})}
,
\overline{D(J_{m})}

do not intersect with
\infty_{1}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}
.

By the assumption
n,m\geq a
, the ideals
(J_{n},x_{1}^{a}),(J_{m},x_{1}^{a})\subset k[x_{1},x_{2}]
contain
x_{2}
and the ideals
(J_{n}^{\prime},\tau_{1}^{a}),(J_{m}^{\prime},\tau_{1}^{a})\subset k[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]
contain
\tau_{2}
, which implies (
3.13
) (without the last term) if
T
is contained in
\overline{D(J_{m})}
or
\overline{D(J_{n})}
.

On the other hand, the ideal
(H,x_{1}^{a})\subset k[x_{1},x_{2},t]
contains

x_{2}
and the ideal
(H^{\prime},\tau_{1}^{a})\subset k[\tau_{1},\tau_{2},t]
contains

\tau_{2}
. Over
\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t}-0=\operatorname{Spec}k[u]
with
u=t^{-1}
,

\overline{D(H)}\cap\big(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{1}}\times(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t}-0)\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{x_{2}}\big)
is the zero divisor of

\tilde{H}=(x_{1}^{n+1}-x_{1}^{m+1})(1-x_{2})+u(x_{1}^{m+1}-1)(x_{1}^{n+1}-x_{2}),

and
\overline{D(H)}\cap\big((\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{1}}-0)\times(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{t}-0)\times(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{x_{2}}-0)\big)
is the zero divisor of

\tilde{H}^{\prime}=(\tau_{1}^{m+1}-\tau_{1}^{n+1})(\tau_{2}-1)+u(1-\tau_{1}^{m+1})(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}^{n+1}),

The ideal
(\tilde{H},x_{1}^{a})\subset k[x_{1},x_{2},u]
contains
ux_{2}
and
the ideal
(\tilde{H}^{\prime},\tau_{1}^{a})\subset k[\tau_{1},\tau_{2},u]
contains
u\tau_{2}
.
This shows (
3.13
) if
T\subset\overline{D(H)}
and completes the proof of the claim.
∎

Lemma 3.11
.

For
n\geq a
we have
\rho_{n}(Z)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
3.10
and (
3.12
).
∎

For an integer
N\geq a
let

\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}^{(N)}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\subset\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})

be the subgroup generated by prime correspondences lying in

\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}^{(N)}((\mathbf{A}^{1}-0)\times X,(\mathbf{A}^{1}-0)\times Y).

By Lemma
3.11
, we get a map for
n\geq N\geq a

(3.14)

\rho_{n}^{(a)}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}^{(N)}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}).

The map (
3.14
) induces a map of cubical complexes

(3.15)

\rho_{n}^{(a)\bullet}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}^{(N)}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{\bullet},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{\bullet},\mathcal{Y}).

By the construction the following diagram is commutative if
n\geq N\geq b\geq a
:

(3.16)

\textstyle{\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}^{(N)}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{\bullet},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{n}^{(a)\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{*}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{\bullet},\mathcal{Y})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}^{(N)}({\overline{\square}}^{(b)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{\bullet},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{n}^{(b)\bullet}}

where
\beta^{*}
is induced by the natural map
\beta:{\overline{\square}}^{(b)}_{red}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}
.

Corollary 3.12
.

For
m,n\geq N\geq a
,
\rho_{n}^{(a)\bullet}
and
\rho_{m}^{(a)\bullet}
are homotopic.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.10
, we get a map

(3.17)

s_{m,n}=\rho_{h}(-\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}):\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}^{(N)}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{Y})

such that
\partial\circ s_{m,n}=\rho_{m}^{(a)}-\rho_{n}^{(a)}
, where

\partial=i_{0}^{*}-i_{1}^{*}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{Y})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}).

Let

s^{i}_{m,n}:\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}^{(N)}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{i},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{i+1},\mathcal{Y})

be the map (
3.17
) defined replacing
\mathcal{X}
by
\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{i}
.
Then we have that

\partial\circ((-1)^{i}s^{i}_{m,n})+(-1)^{i-1}s^{i-1}_{m,n}\circ\partial=\rho_{n}^{(a),i}-\rho_{m}^{(a),i}

hence
\{(-1)^{i}s^{i}_{m,n}\}_{i}
gives the desired homotopy.
∎

Let
Z\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}^{(N)}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})
, then for all
W\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{X})
, by
[
15
, Lemma 4.4]

Z\circ(Id_{\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\}}\otimes W)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}^{(N)}((\mathbf{A}^{1}-0)\times X,(\mathbf{A}^{1}-0)\times Y).

Moreover, by
[
3
, Prop 1.2.4(i)]
we have

Z\circ(Id_{\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\}}\otimes W)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y}),

which implies that

L_{a}(\mathcal{Y})^{(N)}=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}^{(N)}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{Y}))\\
=\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}^{(N)}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes(-),{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})

is an object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
, which is a subobject of

L_{a}(\mathcal{Y})=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{Y}))\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}},

and we have

(3.18)

L_{a}(\mathcal{Y})=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{N>0}L_{a}(\mathcal{Y})^{(N)}.

The above construction gives a map of complexes in

\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
:

\rho^{(a)\bullet}_{N}:C_{\bullet}L_{a}(\mathcal{Y})^{(N)}\to C_{\bullet}(\mathcal{Y}),

where
C_{\bullet}(-)
is the cubical Suslin complex. Let

\rho^{(a)}_{N}:H_{i}(C_{\bullet}L_{a}(\mathcal{Y})^{(N)})\to H_{i}(C_{\bullet}(\mathcal{Y}))

be the map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
induced on cohomology presheaves.
Thanks to Corollary
3.12
, the diagram

\textstyle{H_{i}(C_{\bullet}L_{a}(\mathcal{Y})^{(N)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{(a)}_{N}}
\textstyle{h_{i}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathcal{Y})}
\textstyle{H_{i}(C_{\bullet}L_{a}(\mathcal{Y})^{(N^{\prime})})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{(a)}_{N^{\prime}}}

commutes for integers
N^{\prime}\geq N
. Hence, by (
3.18
) we get maps

\rho^{(a)}:H_{i}(C_{\bullet}L_{a}(\mathcal{Y}))\to h_{i}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathcal{Y}).

Putting
\Phi={\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y}
, we have

C_{\bullet}(L_{a}(\mathcal{Y}))=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{\bullet},\Phi)).

Recall that for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
and
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
, we have by the Hom-tensor adjunction an isomorphism:

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{X}),F)\cong\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{X}),h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(F)).

Hence, we get an isomorphism

H_{0}(C_{\bullet}L_{a}(\mathcal{Y}))\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\Phi)),

where
h_{i}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\Phi)=H_{i}(C_{\bullet}(\Phi))
and we have an isomorphism

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\Phi)=h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})={\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}.

Hence we get a natural map

(3.19)

\rho^{(a)}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\gamma_{a}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y})\to h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathcal{Y}).

where

\gamma_{a}(F):=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red},F)\text{ for }\;\;F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}},

and by abuse of notation, for
C\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}
, we let
C
denote also
\tau_{!}C\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}

(cf. §1(
16
)).
In view of (
3.16
), the following diagram is commutative (recall that we assume
b\geq a
), :

\textstyle{\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\Phi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{(a)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{*}}
\textstyle{h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathcal{Y})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(b)}_{red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\Phi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{(b)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}

Now take any
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
and consider a presentation in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
:

A\to B\to F\to 0,

where
A,B
are the direct sum of
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathcal{Y})
for varying
\mathcal{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
.
We then get a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\gamma_{a}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{(a)}_{A}}
\textstyle{\gamma_{a}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{(a)}_{B}}
\textstyle{\gamma_{a}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where the vertical maps are induced by (
3.19
).
The upper sequence is exact by the right-exactness of
\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}
and the fact that

{\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}
is a projective object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
.
Thus we get the induced map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
:

(3.20)

\rho^{(a)}_{F}:\gamma_{a}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F)\to F.

Write
\rho_{F}=\rho^{(1)}_{F}
.

Claim 3.13
.

The map
\rho_{F}
splits
\iota_{F}
.

Proof.

By the construction of
\rho_{F}
, this is reduced to the case
F=h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\mathcal{Y})
for
\mathcal{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
, which follows from Lemma
3.9
(2).
∎

The following result concludes the proof of Proposition
3.8
:

Lemma 3.14
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
,
\rho_{F}
factors through

\rho_{F}^{sp}:\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}F)\to F^{sp}.

Moreover it splits the map
\iota_{F}^{sp}
from (
3.5
).

Proof.

Take
\mathcal{X}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
and let
\varphi
be in the kernel of

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}F).

Note that the map is surjective since
{\overline{\square}}^{(a)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}
is a projective
object of
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
by Yoneda’s lemma.
By the definition of semi-purification (cf. §1(
14
)),
there exists an integer
m>0
such that

\beta_{m}^{*}\varphi=0\text{ in }\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(m)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(m)},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F),

where
\beta_{m}:{\overline{\square}}^{(m)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(m)}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}
(cf. §1(
6
)).
Then the maps from (
3.20
) induce a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{*}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 70.0pt\rho_{F}}
\textstyle{F(\mathcal{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{m}^{*}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(m)},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 70.0pt\rho_{F}}
\textstyle{F(\mathcal{X}^{(m)})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(m)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(m)},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{(m)}_{F}}

where
\theta_{m}^{*}
is induced by
\theta_{m}:\mathcal{X}^{(m)}\to\mathcal{X}
and the triangle commutes by (
3.16
). We have

\theta_{m}^{*}\rho_{F}(\varphi)=\rho^{(m)}_{F}\beta^{*}_{m}(\varphi)=0.

Hence
\rho_{F}(\varphi)
lies in the kernel of
\theta^{*}_{m}
, which is contained in the kernel of the map

sp_{\mathcal{X}}:F(\mathcal{X})\to F^{sp}(\mathcal{X})

by the definition of semi-purification. Hence the composite map

sp_{\mathcal{X}}\circ\rho_{F}:\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F)\to F^{sp}(\mathcal{X})

factors through
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}F)
inducing the desired map
\rho_{F}^{sp}
.
Finally, to show the last assertion, consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-20.0pt\iota_{F}}
\textstyle{\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 30.0pt\rho_{F}}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{sp}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-20.0pt\iota_{F}^{sp}}
\textstyle{\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 30.0pt\rho_{F}^{sp}}
\textstyle{F^{sp}}

where
\rho_{F}\iota_{F}=id_{F}
by Claim
3.13
.
This implies
\rho_{F}^{sp}\iota_{F}^{sp}=id_{F^{sp}}
since
F\to F^{sp}
is surjective.
This completes the proof of Lemma
3.14
.
∎

4.
Completion of the proof of the main theorem

In this section we prove the following result:

Proposition 4.1
.

For
\varphi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})
with
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
, there exists
f\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
such that
\varphi
and
id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\otimes f
have the same image in

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}\mathcal{Y})
.

First we deduce Theorem
3.4
follows from Proposition
4.1
. By Proposition
3.8
it suffices to show the surjectivity of the map (
3.5
)
\iota_{F}^{sp}
.
Proposition
4.1
implies that the following composition

h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathcal{Y})\to\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y})\to\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}\mathcal{Y})\simeq\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathcal{Y}))

is surjective. Since the last object is semi-pure,
it factors through
h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathcal{Y})^{sp}
, proving the desired surjectivity for

F=h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathcal{Y})
.

For a general
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
consider a surjection

q:\bigoplus_{\mathcal{Y}\to F}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathcal{Y})\to F

which gives a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\bigoplus h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathcal{Y})^{sp}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-20.0pt\oplus\iota_{\mathcal{Y}}^{sp}}
\scriptstyle{q^{sp}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}\mathcal{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{sp}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-20.0pt\iota_{F}^{sp}}
\textstyle{\gamma({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}F)}

where the top arrow is surjective
and the vertical arrows are surjective since representable presheaves are projective objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
by Yoneda’s lemma and the functors
(\_)^{sp}
and
{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\_
commute with direct sums and preserves surjective maps.
This proves the desired surjectivity of
\iota_{F}
.

The proof of Proposition
4.1
requires a construction analogous to the one in
[
16
]
. For a variable
T
over
k
and for
i\geq 1
, we put

{\overline{\square}}^{(i)}_{T}=(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{T},i(0+\infty))

where
\mathbf{P}^{1}_{T}
is the compactification of
\mathbf{G}_{m,T}=\operatorname{Spec}k[T,T^{-1}]
.
We also put (cf. (
2.1
)):

{\overline{\square}}^{(i)}_{T,red}=\operatorname{Ker}\big(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}({\overline{\square}}^{(i)}_{T})\overset{pr}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{Z}=\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\operatorname{Spec}k,\emptyset)\big)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}},

where
pr:\mathbf{P}^{1}_{T}\to\operatorname{Spec}k
is the projection.
Let
e
be the composite of
pr
and
i_{1}:\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T})
induced by
1\in\mathbf{P}^{1}_{T}
. Then
e
is an idempotent of
\mathrm{End}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T})
and

id-e\in\mathrm{End}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T})
, with
id
denoting the identity on
{\overline{\square}}^{(i)}_{T}
, is a splitting of
{\overline{\square}}^{(i)}_{T,red}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(i)}_{T}
. Thus, we get a direct sum decomposition in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
(cf. (
2.1
)):

{\overline{\square}}^{(i)}_{T}={\overline{\square}}^{(i)}_{T,red}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\;\text{ with}\;\;{\overline{\square}}^{(i)}_{T,red}=(id-e){\overline{\square}}^{(i)}_{T}.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
and integers
i_{1},\dots,i_{n}\geq 1
, let

\pi:\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(i_{1})}_{T}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(i_{n})}_{T},F)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(i_{1})}_{T,red}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(i_{n})}_{T,red},F)

be the projection induced by the above decomposition.

For
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and
a\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}^{\times})
, let

[a]\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X,\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\})
be the map given by
z\to a
, where

\mathbf{A}^{1}=\operatorname{Spec}k[z]
.

Lemma 4.2
.

(1)

The correspondences

[T],[U],[TU],[1]\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{T}-\{0\})\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{U}-\{0\}),(\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\}))

lie in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})
.
Moreover we have

[T]+[U]-[TU]-[1]=0\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

(2)

The correspondences

[-T],[-U],[-TU],[-1]\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{T}-\{0\})\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{U}-\{0\}),(\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\}))

lie in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})
.
Moreover we have

[-T]+[-U]-[-TU]-[-1]=0\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

Proof.

The first assertion of (1) follows from the fact

[T]=\mu(id\otimes[1]),\qquad[U]=\mu(id\otimes[1]),\qquad[TU]=\mu

where
\mu:(\mathbf{A}_{T}^{1}-\{0\})\times(\mathbf{A}_{U}^{1}-\{0\})\to(\mathbf{A}_{W}^{1}-\{0\})
is the multiplication
W=TU
, which lies in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{W})
by
[
13
, Claim 1.21]
.

To show the second assertion of (1), consider as in
[
17
, p.142]
the finite correspondence
Z
given by the following algebraic subset:

(4.1)

\{V^{2}-\big(W(T+U)+(1-W)(TU+1)\big)V+TU=0\}\\
\in\mathbf{Cor}((\mathbf{A}_{T}^{1}-\{0\})\times(\mathbf{A}_{U}^{1}-\{0\})\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{W},\mathbf{A}_{V}^{1}-\{0\})

Let

i_{0},i_{1}:(\mathbf{A}_{T}^{1}-0)\times(\mathbf{A}_{U}^{1}-0)\times(\mathbf{A}_{V}^{1}-0)\to(\mathbf{A}_{T}^{1}-0)\times(\mathbf{A}_{U}^{1}-0)\times\mathbf{A}_{W}^{1}\times(\mathbf{A}_{V}^{1}-0)

be the maps induced by the inclusion of
0_{W}
and
1_{W}
in
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{W}
. It is clear that
(i_{0}^{*}-i_{1}^{*})(Z)=([TU]+[1])-([T]+[U])
since

\displaystyle V^{2}-(TU+1)V+TU=(V-TU)(V-1),

\displaystyle V^{2}-(T+U)V+TU=(V-T)(V-U)

We need to check that
Z
lies in

\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U}\otimes{\overline{\square}}_{W},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{V})
.
Consider the compactification
(\mathbf{P}^{1})^{\times 4}
of
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{T}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{U}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{W}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{V}
given coordinates with the usual convention
[0:1]=\infty
and
[1:0]=0
:

([T_{0}:T_{\infty}],[U_{0}:U_{\infty}],[W_{0}:W_{\infty}],[V_{0}:V_{\infty}]).

Then the closure of
Z
is the hypersurface given by the following polyhomogeneous polynomial:

T_{0}U_{0}W_{0}V_{\infty}^{2}-\big(W_{\infty}(T_{0}U_{\infty}+T_{\infty}U_{0})+(W_{0}-W_{\infty})(T_{\infty}U_{\infty}+T_{0}U_{0})\big)V_{\infty}V_{0}\\
+T_{\infty}U_{\infty}W_{0}V_{0}^{2}.

We have to check that it satisfies the modulus condition: letting

\varphi:\overline{Z}\to(\mathbf{P}^{1})^{\times 4}

be the inclusion and letting

D_{1}=(\{0\}+\{\infty\})\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{U}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{W}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{V}+\mathbf{P}^{1}_{T}\times(\{0\}+\{\infty\})\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{W}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{V}+\mathbf{P}^{1}_{T}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{U}\times\{\infty\}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{V},

D_{2}=\mathbf{P}^{1}_{T}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{U}\times\mathbf{P}^{1}_{W}\times(\{0\}+\{\infty\}),

we have to check the following inequality:

(4.2)

\varphi^{*}(D_{1})\geq\varphi^{*}(D_{2}).

Consider the Zariski cover of
(\mathbf{P}^{1})^{\times 4}
given by:

\Bigl\{\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta}=(\mathbf{P}^{1}-\alpha)\times(\mathbf{P}^{1}-\beta)\times(\mathbf{P}^{1}-\gamma)\times(\mathbf{P}^{1}-\delta),\ \alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\in\{0,\infty\}\Bigr\}.

Define
t_{\alpha}=T_{\infty}/T_{0}
if
\alpha=\infty
and
t_{\alpha}=T_{0}/T_{\infty}
if
\alpha=0
and
u_{\beta}
,
w_{\gamma}
,
v_{\delta}
similarly. Then

\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta}=\operatorname{Spec}(k[t_{\alpha},u_{\beta},w_{\gamma},v_{\delta}]).

On this cover, the Cartier divisors
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
are given by the following system of local equations:

D_{1}=\Bigl\{(\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,0,\delta},t_{\alpha}u_{\beta}w_{0}),(\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\infty,\delta},t_{\alpha}u_{\beta})\Bigr\}\qquad D_{2}=\Bigl\{(\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta},v_{\delta})\Bigr\}

The equation of
\overline{Z}
on

(\mathbf{P}^{1})^{\times 4}-\{0\}
is of the form

T_{0}U_{0}W_{0}-v_{0}F,\qquad\textrm{for some }F\in k[v_{0}][U_{0},U_{\infty},\ldots]

Hence (
4.2
) is satisfied on
\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,0}
if
\alpha=0
or
\beta=0
or
\gamma=0
. Furthermore
\overline{Z}\cap\mathcal{U}_{\infty,\infty,\infty,0}\cap D_{2}=\emptyset
. Similarly, the equation of
\overline{Z}
on

(\mathbf{P}^{1})^{\times 4}-\{\infty\}
is of the form

T_{\infty}U_{\infty}W_{0}-v_{\infty}G,\qquad\textrm{for some }G\in k[v_{\infty}][U_{0},U_{\infty},\ldots]

Hence (
4.2
) is satisfied on
\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\infty}
if
\alpha=\infty
or
\beta=\infty
or
\gamma=0
. Furthermore
\overline{Z}\cap\mathcal{U}_{0,0,\infty,\infty}\cap D_{2}=\emptyset
.

(2) is proved by the same argument using the following correspondence instead of
(
4.1
):

\{V^{2}+\big(W(T+U)+(1-W)(TU+1)\big)V+TU=0\}\\
\in\mathbf{Cor}((\mathbf{A}_{T}^{1}-\{0\})\times(\mathbf{A}_{U}^{1}-\{0\})\times\mathbf{A}^{1}_{W},\mathbf{A}_{V}^{1}-\{0\}).

∎

Corollary 4.3
.

\pi([TU])=0\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U,red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}))
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
4.2
since

[TU]\circ\big((id-e)\otimes(id-e)\big)=[TU]-[TU]\circ(1\otimes e)-[TU]\circ(e\otimes 1)+[TU]\circ(e\otimes e)\\
=[TU]-[T]-[U]+[1]\text{ in }\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}).

∎

For
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and
a,b\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}^{\times})
, let

[a,b]\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}(X,(\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\})\otimes(\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\}))

be the map given by
z\to a,\;w\to b
, where
z
(resp.
w
) is the standard coordinate of the first (resp. second)
\mathbf{A}^{1}
.

Corollary 4.4
.

In
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{V},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}))
,
we have:

[T,V]+[U,V]-[TU,V]-[1,V]=[-T,V]+[-U,V]-[-TU,V]-[-1,V]=0.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
4.2
noting the endofunctor
\_\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}
on

\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}
is additive and

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})
is a quotient of
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}
.
∎

Proposition 4.5
.

The correspondences

[U,T],\;[T^{-1},U]\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}((\mathbf{A}^{1}_{T}-\{0\})\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{U}-\{0\}),(\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\})\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\}))

lie in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})
.
Moreover, the element

\pi([U,T])-\pi([T^{-1},U])\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U,red},h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}))

lies in the kernel of the map

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U,red},h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}))\to\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{U,red},h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}))

Proof.

(see
[
16
, Corollary 9]
)
The first assertion is easily checked. To show the second, consider the map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
:

{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{S}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U}\;;\;T\to S,\;U\to S^{-1}.

Composing this with the correspondences of Lemma
4.2
(1), we get

[S]+[S^{-1}]-2[1]=0\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{S,red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

Noting
\pi([1])=(id-e)\circ[1]=0
, we get

\pi([S]+[S^{-1}])=0\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{S,red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

This implies

(4.3)

\pi([S,V]+[S^{-1},V])=0\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{S,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{V,red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

again noting that the endofunctor
\_\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{V}
on
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
is additive and

h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})
is a quotient of
h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)})\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}
.

On the other hand, by tensoring the correspondence of Corollary
4.3
with another copy of itself we get

(4.4)

\pi([TU,VW])=0\\
\text{ in }\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{V,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{W,red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

There is a map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
:

{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{S_{1}}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{S_{2}}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{V}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{W}\;;\;\\
T\to S_{1},\;U\to S_{2},\;V\to-S_{1},\,W\to S_{2},

which induces an element of

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{S_{1},red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{S_{2},red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{V,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{W,red}).

Composing this with (
4.4
) and changing variables
(S_{1},S_{2})
to
(T,U)
,
we get

(4.5)

\pi([TU,-TU])=0\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{U,red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

We make the following claim:

Claim 4.6
.

In
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U,red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}))
,
we have

(4.6)

\pi([TU,-TU])=\pi([T,-TU])+\pi([U,-TU]),

(4.7)

\pi([T,-TU])=\pi([T,U]),

(4.8)

\pi([U,-TU])=\pi([U,T]).

Indeed, composing the first correspondence of Corollary
4.4
with the map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
:

(4.9)

{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{V}

given by
V\to-TU
which is admissible by
[
13
, Claim 1.21]
, we get

[TU,-TU]+[1,-TU]-[T,-TU]-[U,-TU]=0\\
\text{ in }\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

Then (
4.6
) follows from the equality:

\pi([1,-TU])=0\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U,red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

Indeed, we have

[1,-TU]\circ((id-e)\otimes(id-e))=\\
[1,-TU]-[1,-TU]\circ(id\otimes e)-[1,-TU]\circ(e\otimes id)+[1,-TU]\circ(e\otimes e)\\
=[1,-TU]-[1,-T]-[1,-U]+[1,-1]\overset{(*)}{=}0\\
\text{ in }\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{T}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{U},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}),

where the equality
(*)
above follows from Corollary
4.4
.
Then (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) follow from Corollary
4.4
by an analogous argument considering the maps (
4.9
) given by

V\to T,T\to-T
and
V\to U,U\to-U
respectively, and noticing that:

[T,-T]\circ((id-e)\otimes(id-e))=\\
[T,-T]-[T,-T]\circ(id\otimes e)-[T,-T]\circ(e\otimes id)+[T,-T]\circ(e\otimes e)\\
=[T,-T]-[T,-T]-[1,-1]+[1,-1]=0,\\

and similarly for
[U,-U]
. This completes the proof of the claim.

By the above claim, (
4.5
) implies

(4.10)

\pi[T,U]+\pi[U,T]=0\text{ in }\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{U,red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

Putting (
4.3
) and (
4.10
) together we conclude that

\pi[T,U]-\pi[U^{-1},T]=0\text{ in }\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{T,red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{U,red},h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)})).

This completes the proof of Proposition
4.5
.
∎

Take
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
and

\varphi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})

It induces

\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y}).

Let

\varphi^{*}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},\mathcal{Y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})

be obtained from
\varphi
by the obvious permutation.
It induces

\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}}^{*}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},\mathcal{Y}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}).

We then put

\varphi\otimes Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}),

Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\otimes\varphi^{*}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}),

which induce

\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}}\otimes Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}),

Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\otimes\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}}^{*}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}).

For
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
, let

\sigma_{\mathcal{M}}\colon{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{M}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{M}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}

be the permutation of the two copies of
{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}
. We have

\varphi\otimes Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}=(\sigma_{\mathcal{Y}})\circ(Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\otimes\varphi^{*})\circ(\sigma_{\mathcal{X}}).

Let
T
be the standard coordinate on
\mathbf{A}^{1}
and let

(4.11)

\iota:{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}

be the map given by
T\to T^{-1}
. For all
\mathcal{M}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
let

\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathcal{M}}=\sigma_{\mathcal{M}}-Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{M}}\otimes\iota\colon{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{M}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{M}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}.

We can write

\varphi\otimes Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}=Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\otimes\varphi^{*}+(\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y}})\circ p+q\circ(\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}}),

for some

p,q\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}).

Put

\Gamma_{\mathcal{X}}={\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{X}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\qquad\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y}}={\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}.

Hence we can write

(4.12)

\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}}\otimes Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}=Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\otimes\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}}^{*}+\sigma^{\prime}_{{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{Y}}\circ p+q_{{\overline{\square}}}\circ\sigma^{\prime}_{{\overline{\square}},X},

where

\sigma^{\prime}_{{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{Y}}:{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\to\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y}}

\sigma^{\prime}_{{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{X}}:{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\to\Gamma_{\mathcal{X}}

q_{{\overline{\square}}}:\Gamma_{\mathcal{X}}\to\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y}}

are induced by
\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y}}
,
\sigma^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}}
and
q
respectively. For an integer
n>0
let
\mathcal{X}^{(n)}:=(X,nD)
if
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)
.
Then we consider the map

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y}})\xrightarrow{\beta^{*}_{n}}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(n)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red},\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y}})

induced by the natural map

\beta_{n}:{\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(n)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}
.

Claim 4.7
.

There is
N\geq 2
such that for all
n\geq N
the maps
\sigma^{\prime}_{{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{Y}}\circ p
and
q_{{\overline{\square}}}\circ\sigma^{\prime}_{{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{X}}
lie in the kernel of

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y}})\xrightarrow{\beta^{*}_{n}}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(n)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red},\Gamma_{\mathcal{Y}})

Proof.

By Proposition
4.5
, the composite map

{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{red}\overset{\beta_{2}}{\longrightarrow}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\overset{\sigma^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\to h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})

is zero, where

\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma-Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\otimes\iota

with
\sigma
the permutation of the two copies of
{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}
and
\iota
from (
4.11
). This immediately implies the claim for
q_{{\overline{\square}}}\circ\sigma^{\prime}_{{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{X}}
. We now show the claim for
\sigma^{\prime}_{{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{Y}}\circ p
. Choose such an integer
N
that for all
n\geq N
there is map
