p^{(n)}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(n)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y}^{(2)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(2)}_{red})

induced by
p
. For
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
, write

\Lambda_{\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{M}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{N}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}),

\Lambda^{(n)}_{\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N}}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{(n)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{N}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}).

Then we have a commutative diagram for
n\geq N

(4.13)

\textstyle{\Lambda_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{2}^{*}}
\textstyle{\Lambda_{\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{n}^{*}}
\textstyle{\Lambda^{(2)}_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(p^{(n)})^{*}}
\textstyle{\Lambda^{(n)}_{\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}},}

The claim for
\sigma^{\prime}_{{\overline{\square}},\mathcal{Y}}\circ p
follows from this.

∎

We now complete the proof of Proposition
4.1
.
We consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{n}^{*}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathcal{X}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red},\mathcal{Y}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{n}^{*}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(n)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{n}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathcal{X}^{(n)}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red},\mathcal{Y}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})}

where the horizontal maps come from (
3.19
) replacing
\mathcal{Y}
with
\mathcal{Y}\otimes{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}
.
By Lemma
3.9
(3) and (2) we have

\rho_{1}(\varphi\otimes id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}})=\rho(\varphi)\otimes Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\rho_{1}(Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\otimes\varphi^{*})=\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}}^{*},\;\text{ where}

(4.14)

\rho:\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{Y})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathcal{X},h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}(\mathcal{Y}))

is the map from (
3.19
).
In view of the diagram, (
4.12
) and Claim
4.7
imply that there is
n\gg 0
such that

\beta_{n}^{*}(\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}}^{*}-\rho(\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}})\otimes Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}})=0
so that

(4.15)

\beta_{n}^{*}(\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}}-Id_{{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}}\otimes\rho(\varphi_{{\overline{\square}}}))=0\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(n)},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y}).

Consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{*}_{n}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}\mathcal{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{*}_{n}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(n)},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}^{(n)}_{red}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{(n)},{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}\mathcal{Y})}

The two horizontal maps are surjective since representable presheaves are projective objects of
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
and

{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathcal{Y}\to{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}\mathcal{Y}
is surjective.
The map
\beta^{*}_{n}
on the right hand side is injective since
{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{sp}\mathcal{Y}
is semi-pure. Hence Proposition
4.1
follows from (
4.15
).

5.
Implications on reciprocity sheaves

Let
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
be the category of reciprocity sheaves (see §
1

(
17
)).
Recall that for simplicity, we denote for all
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}

(cf. §
1
(
18
))

\widetilde{F}:=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}.

By
[
10
]
there is a
lax
monoidal structure on
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
given by
(cf. Proposition
3.1
)

\bigl(F,G\bigr)_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}:=\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\widetilde{G}).

Following
[
10
, 5.21]
, we define

(5.1)

F\langle 0\rangle:=F,\qquad F\langle n\rangle:=\bigl(F\langle n-1\rangle,\mathbf{G}_{m}\bigr)_{\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}}\text{ for }n\geq 1.

By Corollary
2.2
(1), we have (cf. (
3.3
))

(5.2)

F\langle n\rangle\cong\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F\langle n-1\rangle}(1)).

By recursiveness of the definition we have

(5.3)

\bigl(F\langle n\rangle\bigr)\langle m\rangle\cong F\langle n+m\rangle.

By
[
10
, Prop. 5.6 and Cor. 5.22]
, we have isomorphisms

(5.4)

\underline{\omega}_{!}((\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}n})\cong\mathbb{Z}\langle n\rangle\cong\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},\quad\mathbf{G}_{a}\langle n\rangle\cong\Omega^{n}\;\text{ if }\ ch(k)=0,

where the second isomorphism is defined as follows: for an affine
X=\operatorname{Spec}A\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, the composite map

(5.5)

\mathbf{G}_{a}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbf{G}_{m}(A)^{\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}n}\to(\mathbf{G}_{a}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathbf{G}_{m}^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}n})(A)\to\mathbf{G}_{a}\langle n\rangle(A)\overset{\eqref{KMGatensor}}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{n}_{A}

sends
a\otimes f_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{n}
with
a\in A
and
f_{i}\in A^{\times}
to
a{\operatorname{dlog}}f_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge{\operatorname{dlog}}f_{n}
.

By
[
10
, 5.21 (4)]
, there is a natural surjective map for
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}

(5.6)

F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}\to F\langle n\rangle.

Lemma 5.1
.

The map (
5.6
) factors through a natural surjective map

(5.7)

\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}n})\to F\langle n\rangle.

Proof.

By
[
10
, (5.21.1)]
, there is a natural surjective map

(5.8)

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}n})\to F\langle n\rangle.

By Lemma
1.14
(ii) and (iii), we have a natural isomorphism

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{a}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}n})\simeq\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}n}).

Hence (
5.8
) induces (
5.7
).
We have a surjective map

F\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}\overset{\eqref{KMGatensor}}{\simeq}\underline{\omega}_{!}\widetilde{F}\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}((\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}n})\simeq\\
\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}n}))\to\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}n}).

where the second isomorphism comes from the monoidality of
\underline{\omega}_{!}
(cf. §1(
19
)). By the adjunction from (
1.1
), this induces a surjective map

(5.9)

F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}=a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}(F\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M})\to\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})^{\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}n}).

By the construction of (
5.8
), it is straightforward to check
that (
5.6
) is the composite (
5.7
) and (
5.9
).
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We have a map natural in
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
:

(5.10)

F(X)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),F)\xrightarrow{\_\otimes id_{\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}}}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M})\\
\to\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X)\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},F\langle n\rangle),

where the last map is induced by (
5.6
).
Thus we get a map

(5.11)

\lambda_{F}^{n}:F\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},F\langle n\rangle).

Theorem 5.2
.

For
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, the map
\lambda_{F}^{n}
is an isomorphism.

The proof will be given later. First we prove the following.

Proposition 5.3
.

The map
\lambda_{F}^{n}
is an isomorphism for
n=1
.

Proof.

Note
\mathcal{K}_{1}^{M}=\mathbf{G}_{m}
and that for
F_{1},G_{1},F_{2},G_{2}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
and maps
f:F_{1}\to F_{2}
,
g:G_{1}\to G_{2}
, the diagram

\textstyle{\underline{\omega}_{!}F_{1}\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}G_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{\omega}_{!}f\otimes\underline{\omega}_{!}g}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\underline{\omega}_{!}F_{2}\otimes_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}G_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\underline{\omega}_{!}(F_{1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}G_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{\omega}_{!}(f\otimes g)}
\textstyle{\underline{\omega}_{!}(F_{2}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}G_{2}),}

commutes, where the vertical isomorphisms follow from the monoidality of
\underline{\omega}_{!}
.
Thus, by Lemma
5.1
, (
5.10
) with
n=1
coincides with the composite map:

(5.12)

\displaystyle F(X)

\displaystyle=\underline{\omega}_{!}\widetilde{F}(X)\xrightarrow{\underline{\omega}_{!}(\_\otimes id_{\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}})(X)}\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})(X)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}))

\displaystyle\overset{(*1)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}))

\displaystyle\overset{(*2)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}))

\displaystyle\overset{(*3)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}))

\displaystyle\overset{(*4)}{\simeq}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F\langle 1\rangle)(X)

where
(*1)
is induced by the injective unit map

G\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}G
(
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
) for the adjunction (
1.13
) and it is an isomorphism by Corollary
2.9
and the fact that
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\omega^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
,

(*2)
is given by the fully faithfulness of
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}
and

\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathbf{G}_{m}=\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}
by
[
6
, Lem .2.3.1]
,

(*3)
follows from Lemma
1.3
, and
(*4)
holds by the definition (
5.1
).

This gives a commutative diagram

(5.13)

\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{F}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},F\langle 1\rangle)}
\textstyle{\underline{\omega}_{!}\widetilde{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underline{\omega}_{!}\iota^{1}_{\widetilde{F}}}
\textstyle{\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

where
\iota_{\widetilde{F}}^{1}=(\_\otimes id_{\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}})
is an isomorphism from Corollary
3.6

(using Corollary
2.2
).
This proves the proposition.

∎

For
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
let

(5.14)

\iota_{F,G}:\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle)

be the composite map

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)\overset{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\widetilde{G})\overset{-\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})\overset{\underline{\omega}_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle).

Theorem 5.4
.

For
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
,
\iota_{F,G}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We have isomorphisms (cf. §1 (
18
))

(5.15)

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle)\\
=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}),\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}))\\
\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}))\\
\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}))\\
\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}))),

where the first (resp. second) isomorphism follows from (
1.12
)
(resp. the fact
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\tau_{!}(\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
).
Note that for
H\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau,sp}
, the natural map

H\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}H
is injective.

Hence we get injective maps

(5.16)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}))\\
\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})))\\
\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})))\\
\overset{(*1)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})))\\
\overset{(*2)}{\simeq}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},G\langle 1\rangle)),\\

where the isomorphism
(*1)
comes from Proposition
2.10
and

\underline{\omega}_{!}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\simeq id
(cf. §1 (
18
)) and

(*2)
follows from (
5.2
).
These maps fit into a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\widetilde{G})}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hookrightarrow}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{F,G}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}}
\scriptstyle{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hookrightarrow}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle)}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
(
5.15
)
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},G\langle 1\rangle))}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\widetilde{G})}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\beta}

The two right vertical isomorphisms follow from the full faithfulness of
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}
. The isomorphism
\alpha
(resp.
\beta
) comes from

\iota_{\widetilde{G}}^{1}
from Corollaries
3.6
and
2.2
(resp.
\lambda_{G}^{1}
from Proposition
5.3
).
The squares are commutative by (
5.13
)
noting that the left vertical maps are viewed as inclusions under the identifications

\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},G\langle 1\rangle))\\
\simeq\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red})))

coming from Proposition
2.10
.
This proves that the map
\iota_{F,G}
is an isomorphism as desired.

∎

Corollary 5.5
.

For
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
, there exists a natural injective map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
for internal hom:

(5.17)

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G),

which coincides with the inverse of (
5.14
) on the
k
-valued points.

Proof.

The surjective map
F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathbf{G}_{m}\to F\langle 1\rangle
in
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
from (
5.6
) induces an injective map

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathbf{G}_{m},G\langle 1\rangle)\\
\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m},G\langle 1\rangle)

and the latter is isomorphic to
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)
by Proposition
5.3
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.

Consider the map induced by (
5.6
):

q:\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M})\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},F\langle n\rangle).

The map (
5.11
) is then the composition of
q
and the map

(5.18)

F\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M});\;\;s\mapsto s\otimes id_{\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}}.

On the other hand, we have isomorphisms
\mathcal{K}_{i-1}^{M}\langle 1\rangle\cong\mathcal{K}_{i}^{M}
for all
i\geq 1
by (
5.4
).
Hence the map (
5.17
) for
F=\mathcal{K}_{i-1}^{M}
gives an injective map

(5.19)

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{i}^{M},F\langle i\rangle)\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{i-1}^{M},F\langle i-1\rangle).

Composing (
5.19
) for all
i\leq n
, we get an injective map

(5.20)

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},F\langle n\rangle)\hookrightarrow F

which by definition sends
q(s\otimes id_{\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}})
to
s
for a section
s
of
F
. Hence the composition

F\xrightarrow{\eqref{iotaFGm}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},F\langle n\rangle)\overset{\eqref{eq2;cancelRSCMilnorKn}}{\hookrightarrow}F

is the identity, so (
5.11
) is an isomorphism, which completes the proof of Theorem
5.2
.
∎

Let
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
. By Lemma
1.3
we have a natural isomorphism

\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G)\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,G).

Hence, the unit map
id\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}
from (
1.13
) induces a natural map

(5.21)

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G)\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,G).

It is injective by the semipurity of

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G)
,
and becomes an isomorphism after taking
\underline{\omega}_{!}
.
Moreover the following diagram is commutative:

(5.22)

\textstyle{\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.21
)
\scriptstyle{\hookrightarrow}
\textstyle{\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hookrightarrow}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\underline{\omega}^{*}G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\underline{\omega}^{*}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,G)}

where the isomorphism comes from Lemma
1.2
.

For
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
and
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, we define the following condition:

(\clubsuit)_{X}

The maps (
5.21
) is an isomorphism.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
F,G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
. Assume one of the following:

(a)

G
satisfies
(\clubsuit)_{X}
for any
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.

(b)

G
satisfies
(\clubsuit)_{\operatorname{Spec}(K)}

for any function field
K
over
k
and
F
is the quotient of a direct sum of representable objects.

Then (
5.17
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Assume the condition
(a)
. Letting
\widetilde{G}=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}G
, we have isomorphisms for
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}

(5.23)

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)(X)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,G))\\
\underset{(*1)}{\cong}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,G))\underset{(*2)}{\cong}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G})),

where the isomorphism
(*1)
(resp.
(*2)
) comes from the full faithfullness of
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}

(resp.
(\clubsuit)_{X}
).
Moreover, we have isomorphisms

(5.24)

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G})\underset{(*3)}{\cong}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\widetilde{G}(1)))\\
\cong\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}(1))),

where the isomorphism
(*3)
comes from Corollaries
3.6
and
2.2
.
We also have isomorphisms

(5.25)

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle)(X)=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,G\langle 1\rangle))\\
\underset{(*4)}{\cong}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}),\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}(1)))\\
\underset{(*5)}{\cong}\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}(1)))\\
\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}(1))),

where
(*4)
(resp.
(*5)
) comes from Lemma
1.3

(resp. the adjunction (
1.12
)). These maps fit into a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}))}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
(
5.24
)
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}(1))))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hookrightarrow}
\scriptstyle{(\dagger)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
(
5.23
)
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{F},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}(1)))))}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle)(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
(
5.25
)
\scriptstyle{\hookrightarrow}
(
5.17
)

where the injective map
(\dagger)
comes from the counit map
id\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}
from the adjunction (
1.12
).
The diagram commutes since the map (
5.24
) is induced by the map

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G})\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}(1)))\\
\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset)\otimes\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m})

given by
f\mapsto f\otimes id_{\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}}
, and the map (
5.17
) is induced by the surjection

F\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathbf{G}_{m}\to F\langle 1\rangle
from (
5.6
) and the isomorphism inverse of (
5.11
):

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\otimes\mathbf{G}_{m},G\langle 1\rangle)\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)

given by
f\otimes id_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}\mapsto f
,
and the maps (
5.23
) and
(\dagger)
are inclusions under the identifications

\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}(1))\simeq\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{m}\otimes X,G\langle 1\rangle))\\
\simeq\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}_{!}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\widetilde{G}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}^{{\operatorname{Nis}},sp}{\overline{\square}}^{(1)}_{red}))

coming from Lemma
1.3
and Proposition
2.10
.
This proves that (
5.17
) is an isomorphism.

Next assume the condition
(b)
. In view of Lemma
1.5
, we have

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)
and
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle)
are in
\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
. Hence, by Lemma
1.4
, it is enough to prove that (
5.17
) induces an isomorphism

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F\langle 1\rangle,G\langle 1\rangle)(K)\cong\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(F,G)(K)

for any function field
K
over
k
. This follows from the same computations as above.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
satisfies
(\clubsuit)_{X}
for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.

Proof.

We have

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}F)=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}((X,\emptyset),\underline{\omega}^{*}F)\underset{(*1)}{\cong}\underline{\omega}^{*}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,F)\\
\underset{(*2)}{\cong}\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,F),

where the isomorphism
(*1)
follows from Lemma
1.2

and
(*2)
from the fact that
\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,F)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{HI}}
so that

\underline{\omega}^{*}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(X,F))\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}^{\tau}
by
[
6
, Lem. 2.3.1]
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

If
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
,
\Omega^{i}
satisfies
(\clubsuit)_{X}
for all
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
.

Proof.

Put
\Gamma=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),\Omega^{i})
and

G=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X,\emptyset),\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{i}),\quad G^{*}=\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),\Omega^{i}).

Note that
\Gamma\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{\operatorname{Nis}}
by Lemma
1.5
.
By
[
11
, Cor. 6.8]
, for
\mathcal{Y}=(Y,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
where
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and
D_{{\operatorname{red}}}
is a simple normal crossing divisor, we have

(5.26)

G(\mathcal{Y})=\Gamma(Y\times X,\Omega^{i}(\log D_{red}\times X)((D-D_{{\operatorname{red}}})\times X)).

Hence the conductor
c^{G}
associated to
G
in the sense of
[
11
, Def. 4.14]

is given as follows (note that Lemma
1.3
implies
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}(\Gamma)
under the notation of loc. cite.): Let
\Phi
be as
[
11
, Def. 4.1]
. For

a\in G(L)=H^{0}(X\otimes_{k}L,\Omega^{i})\;\text{ with }L\in\Phi,

put
c_{L}^{G}(a)=0
if
a\in H^{0}(X\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{L},\Omega^{i})
. Otherwise, put

c_{L}^{G}(a)=\min\left\{n\geq 1\mid a\in H^{0}(X\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{L},\frac{1}{t^{n-1}}\cdot\Omega^{i}_{X\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{L}}(\log))\right\},

where
t
is a local paramter of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
and
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{L}}(\log)
is the differential graded subalgebra of
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X\otimes_{k}L}
generated by
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{L}}
and
{\operatorname{dlog}}\;t
(cf.
[
11
, §6.1 6.3]
).
Moreover, one easily sees that for
\mathcal{Y}=(Y,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}Cor}}
as (
5.26
),

G(\mathcal{Y})=\left\{a\in G(Y-D)\mid c_{L}^{G}(a)\leq v_{L}(D)\;\text{ for any }L\in\Phi\right\}

(see
[
11
, Notation 4.2]
for
v_{L}(D)
).
Hence, by
[
11
, Th. 4.15(4)]
, it suffices to show
c^{G^{*}}=c^{G}
.
We know
c^{G^{*}}\leq c^{G}
by loc. cite so that it suffices to show the following: Let
L\in\Phi
and
a\in G(L)
. For
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have

c_{L}^{G^{*}}(a)\leq r\Rightarrow c_{L}^{G}(a)\leq r.

We prove it by the descending induction on
r
.
By
[
11
, Cor. 4.44]
this is reduced to showing the following:
Choose a ring homomorphism
K\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{L}
such that
K\to\mathcal{O}_{L}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}/(t)
is an identity and extend it in the canonical way to
\sigma:K(x)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{L_{x}}
,
where
x
is a variable and
L_{x}={\operatorname{Frac}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}[x]_{(t)}^{h})
. Assume
c_{L}^{G}(a)\leq r+1
.
Then the following implication holds

(5.27)

(a,1-xt^{r})_{L_{x},\sigma}=0\in G(K(x))\,\Rightarrow\,c_{L}^{G}(a)\leq r,

where
(-,-)_{L_{x},\sigma}
is the local symbol for
\Gamma^{i}=\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(X),\Omega^{i})
from
[
11
, §4.3 4.37]
.
Since the local symbol is uniquely determined by
the properties (LS1) - (LS4) from
[
11
, §4.3 4.38]
, we see that it is given by

(a,1-xt^{r})_{L_{x},\sigma}=\operatorname{Res}_{t}(a\;{\operatorname{dlog}}(1-xt^{r})),

where

\operatorname{Res}_{t}:\Gamma^{i+1}(L_{x})=H^{0}(X\otimes_{k}L_{x},\Omega^{i+1})\to\Gamma^{i}(K(x))=H^{0}(X\otimes_{k}K(x),\Omega^{i})

is induced by the residue map

\Omega^{i+1}_{L_{x}}\to\Omega^{i}_{K(x)}
, which is defined using
the isomorphism
L_{x}\simeq K(x)((t))
induced by
\sigma:K(x)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{L_{x}}
.
To prove the implication (
5.27
), we may assume after
replacing
a
by
a-b
for some
b\in\Gamma(L)
with
c_{L}^{G}(b)\leq r
,

a=\frac{1}{t^{r}}\alpha+\beta\frac{dt}{t^{r+1}}\;\;\text{for }\alpha\in H^{0}(X\otimes_{k}K,\Omega^{i}),\;\beta\in H^{0}(X\otimes_{k}K,\Omega^{i-1}).

Then we compute in
H^{0}(X\otimes_{k}K(x),\Omega^{i})

\operatorname{Res}_{t}(a\;{\operatorname{dlog}}(1-xt^{r}))=-rx\alpha+\beta dx.

This shows (
5.27
) and completes the proof.

∎

6.
Internal hom’s for
\Omega^{n}

In this section, we assume
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
. Note that a section of

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})
over
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
is given by a collection of
maps

\varphi_{Y}:H^{0}(Y,\Omega^{n})\to H^{0}(X\times Y,\Omega^{m})\;\text{ for }Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}},

which are natural in
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
. For

(\alpha,\beta)\in H^{0}(X,\Omega^{m-n})\oplus H^{0}(X,\Omega^{m-n-1}),

we define

\varphi_{Y,\alpha,\beta}^{n,m}:H^{0}(Y,\Omega^{n})\to H^{0}(X\times Y,\Omega^{m})\;;\;\omega\to p_{X}^{*}\alpha\wedge p_{Y}^{*}\omega+p_{X}^{*}\beta\wedge p_{Y}^{*}d\omega,

where
p_{X}:X\times Y\to X
and
p_{Y}:X\times Y\to Y
are the projections.
The naturalness of
\varphi_{Y,\alpha,\beta}^{n,m}
in
Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
follows from
[
2
]
.
Thus we get a natural map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
:

(6.1)

\Omega^{m-n}\oplus\Omega^{m-n-1}\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})\;;\;(\alpha,\beta)\to\{\varphi_{Y,\alpha,\beta}^{n,m}\}_{Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}},

where
\Omega^{i}=0
for
i<0
by convention.
Taking the sections over
\operatorname{Spec}k
, we get a natural map

(6.2)

\Phi^{n,m}:\Omega^{m-n}_{k}\oplus\Omega^{m-n-1}_{k}\to\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m}).

We also consider the composite map in
\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}
:

(6.3)

\Omega^{m-n}\overset{\eqref{uPhi}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})\overset{{\operatorname{dlog}}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},\Omega^{m}),

where the second map is induced by the map
{\operatorname{dlog}}:\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}\to\Omega^{n}
.
Taking the sections over
\operatorname{Spec}k
, we get a natural map

(6.4)

\Psi^{n,m}:\Omega^{m-n}_{k}\to\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},\Omega^{m}).

The main result of this subsection is the following.

Theorem 6.1
.

The maps (
6.1
) and (
6.3
) are isomorphisms.

First we prove the following.

Proposition 6.2
.

The maps (
6.2
) and (
6.4
) are isomorphisms.

This follows from Lemmas

6.3
,
6.4
and
6.5
below, in light of Theorem
5.4
.
For
i\geq 0
, let us fix the isomorphisms

(6.5)

\sigma^{i}:\Omega^{i-1}\langle 1\rangle\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{i},\;\;\varsigma^{i}:\mathcal{K}_{i-1}^{M}\langle 1\rangle\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{K}_{i}^{M}

coming from (
5.3
) and (
5.4
)

Lemma 6.3
.

(1)

The following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\Omega^{m-n}_{k}\oplus\Omega^{m-n-1}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{n,m}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{n-1,m-1}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n-1},\Omega^{m-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.14
)
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n-1}\langle 1\rangle,\Omega^{m-1}\langle 1\rangle)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the right vertical map is induced by
\sigma^{m}
and
(\sigma^{n})^{-1}
of (
6.5
).

(2)

The following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\Omega^{m-n}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{n,m}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{n-1,m-1}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},\Omega^{m})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n-1}^{M},\Omega^{m-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
5.14
)
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n-1}^{M}\langle 1\rangle,\Omega^{m-1}\langle 1\rangle)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the right vertical map is induced by
\sigma^{m}
and
(\varsigma^{n})^{-1}
of (
6.5
).

Proof.

By
[
10
, Cor. 5.22]
, for an affine
X=\operatorname{Spec}A\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and
i\geq 0
, the composite map

\theta^{i}:\Omega^{i-1}_{A}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A^{\times}\to(\Omega^{i-1}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{NST}}}\mathbf{G}_{m})(A)\overset{\eqref{FGm}}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{i-1}\langle 1\rangle(A)\overset{\sigma^{i}}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{i}_{A}

sends
\omega\otimes f
with
\omega\in\Omega^{i-1}_{A}
and
f\in A^{\times}
to

\omega\wedge{\operatorname{dlog}}f
. Moreover, for
\varphi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n-1},\Omega^{m-1})
and
\varphi^{\prime}=\sigma^{m}\circ\varphi\langle 1\rangle\circ(\sigma^{n})^{-1}
, the diagram

\textstyle{\Omega^{n-1}_{A}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\qquad\theta^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi\otimes id_{A^{\times}}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{n}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{m-1}_{A}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}A^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\qquad\theta^{m}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{m}_{A}}

is commutative. Hence (1) follows from the equation

\alpha\wedge(\omega\wedge{\operatorname{dlog}}f)+\beta\wedge d(\omega\wedge{\operatorname{dlog}}f)=(\alpha\wedge\omega+\beta\wedge d\omega)\wedge{\operatorname{dlog}}f,

where
\alpha\in\Omega^{m-n}_{k}
and
\beta\in\Omega^{m-n-1}_{k}
.

(2) follows from (1) and the commutativity of the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{K}_{n-1}^{M}\langle 1\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{dlog}}\langle 1\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\varsigma^{n}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{n-1}\langle 1\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{n}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{dlog}}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{n}}

which can be verified using (
5.5
).
∎

Lemma 6.4
.

For an integer
n\geq 1
, we have

(6.6)

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\mathbf{G}_{a})=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},\mathbf{G}_{a})=0.

Proof.

We have isomorphisms

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\mathbf{G}_{a})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\underline{\omega}_{!}(\widetilde{\Omega^{n-1}}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m}),\mathbf{G}_{a})\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{\Omega^{n-1}}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathbf{G}_{a})\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{\Omega^{n-1}}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathbf{G}_{a})\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\widetilde{\Omega^{n-1}},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathbf{G}_{a})).\\

where the first isomorphism is induced by
(\sigma^{n})^{-1}
, inverse of (
6.5
), and the second from
(
1.12
).
Similarly we have an isomorphism using
(\varsigma^{n})^{-1}
instead of
(\sigma^{n})^{-1}

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n},\mathbf{G}_{a})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathcal{K}^{M}_{n-1},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathbf{G}_{a})).

We compute

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathbf{G}_{a})(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\underline{\omega}^{*}\mathbf{G}_{m},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathbf{G}_{a})(K(X))\\
\simeq\operatorname{Coker}\big(\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathbf{G}_{a}(K(X))\to\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\mathbf{G}_{a}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K(X)},0+\infty)\big)\\
\simeq\operatorname{Coker}\big(K(X)\to H^{0}(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K(X)},\mathcal{O})\big)=0\\

where the first map is injective by
[
13
, Corollary 0.3]
, and the first (resp. last) isomorphism follows from Corollary
2.2
(1) (resp.
[
11
, Cor. 6.8]
).
This completes the proof of Lemma
6.4
.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

The maps (
6.2
) and (
6.4
) are isomorphisms for
n=0
.

Proof.

The assertion for (
6.4
) is obvious since
\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M}=\mathbb{Z}
for
n=0
.
We prove it for (
6.2
).
We have isomorphisms

(6.7)

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}),\Omega^{i})\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}(h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}),\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{i})\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}({\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}},\omega^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{i})\\
\simeq\operatorname{Ker}\big(H^{0}(\mathbf{P}^{1},\Omega^{i}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(\log\infty)(\infty))\overset{i_{0}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{i}_{k}\big),\\

where the first (resp. last) isomorphism follows from (
1.17
)
(resp.
[
11
, Cor. 6.8]
).
Since
\Omega^{i}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}/k}(\log\infty)=0
for
i>1
,

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(\log\infty)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}
and
\Omega^{1}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}/k}(\log\infty)=\Omega^{1}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}/k}(\infty)
, the standard exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}\otimes_{k}\Omega^{1}_{k}\to\Omega^{1}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(\log\infty)\to\Omega^{1}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}/k}(\log\infty)\to 0

induces an exat sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(\infty)\otimes_{k}\Omega^{i}_{k}\to\Omega^{i}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(\log\infty)(\infty)\to\Omega^{1}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}/k}(2\infty)\otimes_{k}\Omega_{k}^{i-1}\to 0,

where
\Omega^{i-1}_{k}=0
if
i=0
by convention.
Letting
t
be the standard coordinate of
\mathbf{A}^{1}\subset\mathbf{P}^{1}
, we have

H^{0}(\mathbf{P}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(\infty))=k\cdot 1\oplus k\cdot t,\quad H^{0}(\mathbf{P}^{1},\Omega^{1}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}/k}(2\infty))=k\cdot dt,

and
dt
lifts canonically to a section
dt\in H^{0}(\mathbf{P}^{1},\Omega^{1}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(\log\infty)(\infty))
. Hence we get an isomorphism

(6.8)

H^{0}(\mathbf{P}^{1},\Omega^{i}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(\log\infty)(\infty))\simeq(k\cdot 1\oplus k\cdot t)\otimes_{k}\Omega^{i}_{k}\oplus(k\cdot dt)\otimes_{k}\Omega^{i-1}_{k}.

Thus the last group of (
6.7
) is isomorphic to

k\cdot t\otimes_{k}\Omega^{i}_{k}\oplus k\cdot dt\otimes_{k}\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\simeq\Omega^{i}_{k}\oplus\Omega^{i-1}_{k}.

Hence, from (
6.7
), we get a natural isomorphism

(6.9)

\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\oplus\Omega^{i}_{k}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i}).

Next we claim that the map (
6.9
) coincides with (
6.2
) for
n=0
. By Lemma
1.8
(2), we have a commutative diagram

(6.10)

\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}_{a}}
\textstyle{\omega_{!}\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{P}^{1},2\infty)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
1.17
)

where
\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}
is given by
t\in\mathbf{G}_{a}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})=k[t]
. The standard isomorphism

\Omega^{i}(\mathbf{A}_{t}^{1})\simeq(\Omega^{i}_{k}\otimes_{k}k[t])\oplus(\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\otimes_{k}k[t]dt)

induces a natural isomorphism

(6.11)

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}),\Omega^{i})=\Omega^{i}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})\simeq\Omega^{i}_{k}[t]\oplus\Omega^{i-1}_{k}[t]\wedge dt,

where

\Omega^{i}_{k}[t]=\underset{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}{\bigoplus}\Omega^{i}_{k}\cdot t^{m},\quad\Omega^{i-1}_{k}[t]\wedge dt=\underset{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}{\bigoplus}\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\wedge t^{m}dt.

The map
\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}
induces the inclusion

\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}^{*}:\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t}),\Omega^{i})=\Omega^{i}(\mathbf{A}_{t}^{1})

such that

(6.12)

\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}^{*}(\varphi)=\varphi_{\mathbf{A}_{t}^{1}}(t)\text{ for }\;\;\varphi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i}),

where
\varphi_{\mathbf{A}_{t}^{1}}:\mathbf{G}_{a}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{t})=k[t]\to\Omega^{i}(\mathbf{A}_{t}^{1})
is induced by
\varphi
. The following claim follows from (
6.7
), (
6.8
) and (
6.10
).

Claim 6.6
.

The image of
\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}^{*}
is identified under (
6.11
) with

\Omega^{i}_{k}\cdot t\oplus\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\wedge dt\subset\Omega^{i}_{k}[t]\oplus\Omega^{i-1}_{k}[t]\wedge dt,

and the composite map

\Omega^{i}_{k}\oplus\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\overset{\eqref{HomGHaOmega}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})\overset{\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{i}_{k}\cdot t\oplus\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\wedge dt

is given by the obvious identifications

\Omega^{i}_{k}=\Omega^{i}_{k}\cdot t
and
\Omega^{i-1}_{k}=\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\wedge dt
.

Let

(6.13)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})\subset\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})

be the subgroup of
\mathbf{G}_{a}
-linear morphisms.
There is a natural isomorphism

\xi:\Omega^{i}_{k}\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})\;;\quad\omega\mapsto\{\lambda\mapsto\lambda\omega\}\;(\lambda\in\mathbf{G}_{a}).

(
6.13
) is a direct summand since we have a splitting given by

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})\;;\;\varphi\mapsto\{\lambda\mapsto\lambda\varphi(1)\}.

The other summand is

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})^{0}:=\{\varphi|\;\varphi(1)=0\}.

There is a natural map

\xi^{\prime}:\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\to\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})^{0}\;;\quad\omega\mapsto\{\alpha\mapsto\omega\wedge d\alpha\}.

By (
6.12
), under the identification (
6.11
), we have

\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}^{*}(\xi(\omega))=\omega\cdot t,\;\;\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}^{*}(\xi^{\prime}(\eta))=\eta\wedge dt\quad(\omega\in\Omega^{i},\;\eta\in\Omega^{i-1}).

Hence the composite map

\Omega^{i}_{k}\oplus\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\overset{\xi\oplus\xi^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{G}_{a},\Omega^{i})\overset{\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}^{*}}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{i}_{k}\cdot t\oplus\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\wedge dt

is given by the obvious identifications

\Omega^{i}_{k}=\Omega^{i}_{k}\cdot t
and
\Omega^{i-1}_{k}=\Omega^{i-1}_{k}\wedge dt
.
By Claim
6.6
this proves the desired claim and completes the proof of Lemma
6.5
.
∎

To deduce Theorem
6.1
from Proposition
6.2
,
we need some preliminaries.

Let

be the function field of

and

\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}_{K}
,

\operatorname{\mathbf{Cor}}
,

. We have then a map

r_{K}:\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}_{K}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})(K)\;;\;\varphi\to\{\psi_{Y}\}_{Y\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}},

where the second map is

(note

)
and the first is the pullback by the projection

.
Similarly we can define a map

r_{K}:\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}_{K}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},\Omega^{m})\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},\Omega^{m})(K).

By definitions, the following diagrams are commutative.

\textstyle{\Omega^{m-n}_{K}\oplus\Omega^{m-n-1}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

)
(

)
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}_{K}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})(K)}

\textstyle{\Omega^{m-n}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
6.4
)
(
6.3
)
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}_{K}}(\mathcal{K}_{n}^{M},\Omega^{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

In view of Lemma

Lemma 6.7
.

The maps (
6.14
) and (
6.15
) are isomorphisms.

For the proof we need the following. Recall from Conventions that for
U=\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{i}U_{i}\in\widetilde{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}}
and
F\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
we let

F(U):=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}F(U_{i}).

In general for
(Y,D_{Y})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
and
F\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
, we let

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(U,F)(Y,D_{Y}):=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}F(U_{i}\times_{k}Y,U_{i}\times_{k}D_{Y}),

and for
G\in\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}
we have

\operatorname{Hom}(G,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}(U,F))=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}\operatorname{Hom}(G,\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}(U_{i},F)).

Lemma 6.8
.

For
\mathcal{X}=(X,D)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}
and
\mathcal{X}_{K}=(X_{K},D_{K})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}(K)
with
X_{K}=X\times_{k}K
and
D_{K}=D\times_{k}K
, we have a natural isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}_{K}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{X}_{K}),\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}_{K}}\Omega^{n})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathcal{X}),\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(K,\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{n})).

Proof.

By
[
3
, Pr. 1.9.2 c)]
and resolutions of singularities (recall that we are assuming
{\operatorname{ch}}(k)=0
) we may assume
X\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
and
D_{{\operatorname{red}}}
is a simple normal crossing divisor.
From the explicit computation of
\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{n}
in
[
11
, Cor. 6.8]
,

(\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}_{K}}\Omega^{n})(X_{K},D_{K})=H^{0}(X_{K},\Omega^{n}_{X_{K}}(\log(D_{K}))(D_{K}-D_{K,red}))\\
=(\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{n})(X_{K},D_{K}):=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{U\subset S}(\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{n})(X\times_{k}U,D\times_{k}U),

where
U
ranges over the open subsets of
S
.
This proves the lemma.
∎

We now prove Lemma
6.7
.
We only prove the assertion for (
6.14
). The proof for (
6.15
) is similar. Put

{\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}}={\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}\otimes_{\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}}{\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}^{\otimes n},

where
{\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}
and
{\overline{\square}}_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
are from Lemma
1.8
.
By (
1.16
) and (
1.17
) and
[
10
, Theorem 5.20]
,
we have an isomorphism in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
:

(6.16)

a^{V}_{\operatorname{Nis}}\omega_{!}h_{0}^{\overline{\square}}({\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Omega^{n}.

Let
{\overline{\square}}_{K}=(\mathbf{P}^{1}_{K},\infty)\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MCor}}_{K}
and
{\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n},K}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{MPST}}_{K}
be
defined as
{\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}}
.
We have isomorphisms

(6.17)

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}_{K}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}_{K}}(\omega_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}_{K}}({\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n},K}),\Omega^{m})\simeq\\
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}_{K}}({\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n},K},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}_{K}}\Omega^{m})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(K,\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{m})),

where the last isomorphism comes from Lemma
6.8
.
On the other hand, by (
6.16
) and Lemma
1.5
, we have

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}(\mathbb{Z}_{{\operatorname{tr}}}(U),\Omega^{m})\in\operatorname{\mathbf{RSC}}_{{\operatorname{Nis}}}
for
U\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
. Hence, writing
\operatorname{Spec}(K)=\operatornamewithlimits{\varprojlim}_{i}U_{i}
with
U_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{Sm}}
, we have isomorphisms (see Conventions)

(6.18)

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})(K)=\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(U_{i},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m}))\simeq\\
\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(U_{i},\Omega^{m}))\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{\varinjlim}_{i}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\omega_{!}h_{0}^{{\overline{\square}}}({\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}}),\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(U_{i},\Omega^{m}))\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(K,\Omega^{m})).

Hence Lemma
6.7
follows from Lemma
5.8
and the following.

Claim 6.9
.

The following diagram is commutative.

(6.19)

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}_{K}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
6.17
)
\scriptstyle{r_{K}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(K,\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{m}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
6.18
)
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(K,\Omega^{m}))}

where the right vertical map is induced by the map (
5.21
).

To show the above claim, write
\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}}=\mathbf{A}^{1}\times(\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\})^{n}
and

\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K}=\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}}\otimes_{k}K
.
Take the standard coordinates
y
on
\mathbf{A}^{1}
and
(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
on

(\mathbf{A}^{1}-\{0\})^{n}
so that

\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}}=\operatorname{Spec}k[y,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}][x_{1}^{-1},\dots.x_{n}^{-1}].

By the definition of
{\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}}
, we have natural maps in
\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}

(6.20)

\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}},\emptyset)\to(\mathbf{P}^{1},2\infty)\otimes(\mathbf{P}^{1},0+\infty)^{\otimes n}\to{\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}},

which induces a map in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}
:

(6.21)

\lambda_{\Omega^{n}}:\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}})\to\omega_{!}{\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}}\to\Omega^{n},

where the last map is induced by (
6.16
). Let

(6.22)

\lambda_{\Omega^{n},K}:\mathbb{Z}_{\operatorname{tr}}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K})\to\Omega^{n}

be defined as (
6.21
) replacing
k
by
K
.
By the definition of
\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
and
\lambda_{\mathbf{G}_{a}}
(cf. Lemma
1.8
)
and (
5.5
),
\lambda_{\Omega^{n}}
corresponds to

(6.23)

\omega_{0}:=y\frac{dx_{1}}{x_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\frac{dx_{n}}{x_{n}}\in\Omega^{n}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}}).

The map (
6.20
) induces injective maps

(6.24)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}(K,\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\Omega^{m}))\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K},\Omega^{m}),

(6.25)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{\mathbf{\underline{M}PST}}}({\overline{\square}}_{\Omega^{n}},\underline{\omega}^{\operatorname{\mathbf{CI}}}\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(K,\Omega^{m}))\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K},\Omega^{m}),

which are compatible with the right vertical map in (
6.19
)
since applying
\underline{\omega}_{!}
, the map (
5.21
) is identified with the identity on

\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(K,\Omega^{m})
via the isomorphism in Lemma
1.3
.
Hence it suffices to show the commutativity of the diagram

(6.26)

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}_{K}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{r_{K}}
\textstyle{H^{0}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K},\Omega^{m})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}

where
\alpha
(resp.
\beta
) is the composite of (
6.17
) and (
6.24
) (resp. (
6.18
) and (
6.25
)).
By the definition,
\alpha
is induced by
the map
\lambda_{\Omega^{n},K}
from (
6.22
).
As
\lambda_{\Omega^{n},K}
is given by the image
\omega_{0,K}
of
\omega_{0}
from (
6.23
) under the pullback map

p^{*}:\Omega^{n}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}})\to\Omega^{n}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K})
, we have

\alpha(\varphi)=\varphi_{\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K}}(\omega_{0,K})\text{ for }\;\;\varphi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}_{K}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m}),

where

\varphi_{\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K}}:\Omega^{n}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K})\to\Omega^{m}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K})
is induced by
\varphi
.
On the other hand, by the definition of
\beta
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{0}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K},\Omega^{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(K,\Omega^{m}))}
\textstyle{\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(K,\Omega^{m}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\Omega^{n}}^{*}}

where
\lambda_{\Omega^{n}}^{*}
is induced by
\lambda_{\Omega^{n}}
from (
6.21
). Hence we have

\beta(\psi)=\psi_{\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}}}(\omega_{0})\text{ for }\;\;\psi\in\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})(K),

where

\psi_{\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}}}:\Omega^{n}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}})\to\operatorname{\underline{Hom}}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}}(K,\Omega^{m})(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}})=\Omega^{m}(\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K})
is induced by
\psi
. Then, for

\varphi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{{\operatorname{\mathbf{PST}}}_{K}}(\Omega^{n},\Omega^{m})
, we get

\beta(r_{K}(\varphi))=r_{K}(\varphi)_{\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n}}}(\omega_{0})=\varphi_{\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K}}(p^{*}\omega_{0})=\varphi_{\mathbf{A}_{\Omega^{n},K}}(\omega_{0,K})=\alpha(\varphi),

which proves the commutativity of (
6.26
). This concludes the proof of Claim
6.9
, and hence of Lemma
6.8
and therefore also the proof of Theorem
6.1
.
